===== PAGE 5 =====

 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
About This Manual 
This is the Runtime Library Release 3.0.3 version of the PlayStation®2 EE Library Reference - Device 
Libraries manual.  
The purpose of this manual is to define all available PlayStation®2 EE device library structures and 
functions. The companion PlayStation®2 EE Library Overview - Device Libraries describes the structure 
and purpose of the libraries. 
Changes Since Last Release 
Chapter 6: Memory Card Library 
• Description of the following functions have been added. 
  sceMcReadFast()      high-speed reading of files 
  sceMcWriteFast()     high-speed writing to files 
• In the Return Value section of sceMcRead(), the sceMcErrSifRpc value was incorrectly listed as -99, 
but has now been corrected to -91.   
 
Chapter 7: Memory Card Library 2 
• Descriptions of the following  functions have been added. 
  sceMc2ReadFastFile()        high-speed writing to files 
  sceMc2WriteFastFile()       high-speed writing to files 
  sceMc2ReadFastFileAsync()  high-speed reading of files (asynchronous) 
  sceMc2WriteFastFileAsync()  high-speed writing to files (asynchronous) 
Related Documentation 
Library specifications for the IOP can be found in the PlayStation®2 IOP Library Reference manuals and the 
PlayStation®2 IOP Library Overview manuals. 
Note: the Developer Support Web site posts current developments regarding the Libraries and also 
provides notice of future documentation releases and upgrades. 
Typographic Conventions 
Certain Typographic Conventions are used throughout this manual to clarify the meaning of the text: 
Convention Meaning 
courier Indicates literal program code. 
italic Indicates names of arguments and structure 
members (in structure/function definitions only). 
medium bold Indicates data types and structure/function 
names (in structure/function definitions only). 
blue Indicates a hyperlink.

 Device Control Library - Initialization/Termination Functions    1-3 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Initialization/Termination Functions 
sceDbcInit 
Initialize device control library 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libdbc 2.4 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceDbcInit( void )  
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function initializes the device control library. 
Before initializing the library, dbcman.irx must be loaded in the IOP. 
Return value 
Table 1-1 
Value Result 
1 Initialization succeeded 
Other than 1 Initialization failed

 Device Control Library - Library System Function    1-5 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Library System Function 
sceDbcGetErxEntries 
Get library entries 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libdbc 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
void *sceDbcGetErxEntries (void);  
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets a pointer to the SceErxLibraryHeader structure which is needed to register the libdbc 
library in liberx. 
If the SceErxLibraryHeader structure returned by this function is passed to the 
sceErxRegisterLibraryEntries() function of liberx, the statically linked libdbc library can be called from an ERX 
module (can be dynamically linked).  
When this function is linked, the entire implementation of the libdbc library will be linked via the library entry 
table of liberx. 
In some cases, this may also increase the size of the program. 
Return value 
The entry library for libdbc is returned. 
See also 
sceErxInit()

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Structures    2-3 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Structures 
sceCdCLOCK                                                     
Structure which stores the date and time 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified  
libcdvd 1.5 January 22, 2001 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
      u_char stat; 0: normal. Any other: error (e.g. internal battery is dead) 
      u_char second; Second (BCD value) 
      u_char minute; Minute (BCD value) 
      u_char hour; Hour (BCD value) 
      u_char pad; Padding data produced by alignment 
      u_char day; Day (BCD value) 
      u_char month; Month (BCD value) 
      u_char year; Year (BCD value) 
} sceCdCLOCK;  
Description 
Stores the date and time with a BCD value.      
See also 
sceCdReadClock()

2-4    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Structures

sceCdlFILE 
File descriptor (for both CD/DVD) 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.1 July 2, 2001 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
     u_int lsn; Logical sector number of file 
     u_int size; File size (in bytes) 
     char name[16]; Filename 
     u_char date[8]; 1
st
: Seconds 
2
nd
: Minutes 
3
rd
: Hours 
4
th
: Date 
5
th
: Month 
6
th
 7
th
: Year (4 digits) 
     u_int flag; Bits 0-7 are the ISO9660 file flag; other bits are reserved 
} sceCdlFILE;  
Description 
Structure representing CD(DVD)-ROM file position and size. 
See also 
sceCdSearchFile()

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Structures    2-5

sceCdlLOCCD 
CD-ROM read location 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.1 December 23, 1999 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
     u_char minute; Minutes 
     u_char second; Seconds 
     u_char sector; Sector 
     u_char track; Track number 
} sceCdlLOCCD;  
Description 
Structure representing read position (head position) on the CD-ROM. 
Notes 
Provided solely to calculate the CD read location using minutes/seconds/sectors. 
See also 
sceCdIntToPos(), sceCdPosToInt()

2-6    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Structures 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdRMode 
CD(DVD)-ROM read mode 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.1 July 14, 2003 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
     u_char trycount; Read try count (No. of error retries + 1) (0: 256 tries) 
     u_char spindlctrl; SCECdSpinStm:  
Recommended stream rotation speed. 
SCECdSpinNom:  
Starts reading data at maximum rotational velocity and if a 
read error occurs, the rotational velocity is reduced. 
SCECdSpinDvdDL0: 
DVD_Dual:Layer0 read velocity 
SCECdSpinX1:                                                         
Rotate at the standard speed for CD-DA reads 
     u_char datapattern; SCECdSecS2048: Data size 2048 bytes                       
(for PlayStation 2 CD/DVD) 
SCECdSecS2352: Data size 2352 bytes (CD-DA) 
     u_char pad; Padding data produced by alignment 
} sceCdRMode;  
Description 
This structure is used to specify the CD(DVD)-ROM read mode.  
For reading DVD media, the only valid setting for datapattern is SCECdSecS2048. For reading CD-DA 
media, the only valid setting is SCECdSecS2352. 
See also 
sceCdRead() 
 

===== PAGE 19 =====

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Structures    2-7 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdStmInit  
File I/O functions: Stream initialization structure 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.4 October 11, 2001 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
     u_int bufmax; Capacity of stream buffer, in its entirety  
(in number of 2048-byte sectors) 
     u_int bankmax; Number of subdivisions of the stream buffer (i.e. number 
of ring buffers) For a buffer that has been subdivided into 
3 more parts, the desired buffer size is approximately 16 
sectors. 
     u_int iop_bufaddr; IOP memory address of stream buffer 
} sceCdStmInit;  
Description 
This structure is used to specify initial values of the stream for the sceDevctl() file I/O functions. 
See also 
CDIOC_STREAMINIT 
 
 

===== PAGE 20 =====

2-8    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Initialization Function 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Initialization Function 
sceCdInit 
Initialize the CD(DVD)-ROM subsystem 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.1 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdInit (  
 int init_mode) Library initialization mode 
SCECdINIT:   Initialize library and block until commands 
                  can be issued. 
SCECdINoD: Initialize library only 
SCECdEXIT:  Close library 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
Initializes the CD(DVD)-ROM subsystem. 
The CD(DVD-ROM) subsystem must first be initialized even before reading data using stdio (sceRead(), 
etc.), rather than libcdvd functions (sceCdRead(), etc.). 
Notes  
After performing initialization with sceCdInit(), be sure to call sceCdMmode() to specify the type of media 
(CD or DVD). 
The CDIOC_INIT devctl command can be substituted for sceCdInit(). Similarly, CDIOC_MMODE can be 
substituted for sceCdMmode(). Doing this allows the CD(DVD)-ROM to be used with only stdio and without 
using libcdvd at all. 
If this function is used when cdvdman.irx and cdvdfsv.irx have not been replaced within the IOP default 
module, 2 is returned. 
Return value 
Table 2-1 
Value Result 
0 Initialization failed. 
1 Initialization was performed normally. 
2 Although initialization was perf ormed, the default module was 
detected on the IOP side. 
See also 
sceCdMmode() 

===== PAGE 21 =====

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Library System Functions   2-9 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Library System Functions 
sceCdChangeThreadPriority 
      Change the IOP thread priority of an EE-side request processing module 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.0 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceCdChangeThreadPriority(  
 int priority) Value of IOP thread priority for EE-side request 
processing module 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function sets the IOP thread priority of an EE-side request processing module. 
The default value for IOP thread priority of an EE-side request processing module is 81. 
When changing the IOP thread priority, careful consideration must be given to the priorities of other 
modules. Therefore, the IOP thread priority value should not be changed carelessly. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 0. 
If there is an error, returns the KernelErrorCode value from the IOP.      

===== PAGE 22 =====

2-10    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Library System Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdDriveCheckForErxLoading 
Get drive status and media type 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdDriveCheckForErxLoading (  
int *rdy, Pointer to variable for getting drive status. 
SCECdComplete OK to issue command  
SCECdNotReady Drive not ready to receive 
command 
int *dtype) Pointer to variable for getting status of media in drive 
SCECdNODISC No disc mounted 
SCECdDETCT Analyzing disc 
SCECdUNKNOWN Disc could not be recognized 
SCECdPSCD Disc is a PlayStation CD 
SCECdPSCDDA Disc is a PlayStation CD 
(with CD-DA) 
SCECdPS2CD Disc is a PlayStation 2 CD 
SCECdPS2CDDA Disc is a PlayStation 2 CD 
(with CD-DA) 
SCECdPS2DVD Disc is a PlayStation 2 DVD 
SCECdCDDA Disc is a music CD 
SCECdDVDV Disc is DVD video 
 
SCECdIllgalMedia Disc cannot be played 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets the status of the drive and the media format, and writes them to the storage locations 
pointed to by the arguments. 
This function is intended to be used exclusively for determining whether or not an ERX module can be 
loaded from the media before a module is replaced. 
Return value 
When the function completes successfully, 1 is returned. 
If an error occurs, 0 is returned.

Get library entries 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified

void *sceCdGetErxEntries (void);

Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state)

This function gets a pointer to the SceErxLibraryHeader structure which is needed to register libcdvd in 
liberx. 
If the SceErxLibraryHeader structure returned by this function is passed to the 
sceErxRegisterLibraryEntries() function of liberx, the statically linked libcdvd library can be called from an

When this function is linked, the entire implementation of libcdvd will be linked via the library entry table of 
liberx. 
In some cases, this may also increase the size of the program. 
Return value

See also 
sceErxInit()

2-12    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Library System Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdMmode              
Specify the media for reading 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.0 April 28, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdMmode(  
 int media) Read media 
SCECdCD   Specify CD as the read media. 
SCECdDVD         Specify DVD as the read media. 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function is used to specify the read media for the CD(DVD)-ROM subsystem. 
Notes  
This function must be used to specify the read media after the sceCdInit() function is called. 
You must specify a constant for the argument of this function. Otherwise, if you use sceCdGetDiskType() to 
determine the media type dynamically and use the result as the argument of this function, you eliminate the 
effectiveness of using this function as a way of preventing pirated copies from being made. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 1. 
If there is an error, returns 0. 
See also 
sceCdInit() 
 

===== PAGE 25 =====

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Library System Functions   2-13 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdReadDvdDualInfo 
Read dual-layer DVD information 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.5 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdReadDvdDualInfo(  
 int *OnDual, Returns 1 when a dual-layer DVD is detected, otherwise returns 0 
Note: Also returns 1 for DVD-Rs created as dual-layer DVDs 
 u_int *layer1_start) Returns the starting LSN (logical sector number) of layer 1 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
Reads the layer structure information of a dual-layer DVD from the media and sets up the environment. You 
must call this function when you use one layer of a DVD-R to develop a dual-layer DVD. Because this 
function blocks, there is no need to wait for it to complete by calling the sceCdSync() function. Error 
information during reads can be obtained using the sceCdGetError() function. 
Return value 
Returns 1 when successful. 
Returns 0 if an error occurred. 
After this function is successfully invoked, the following error information can be obtained by first waiting for 
the command to complete using a function such as sceCdSync(), then calling sceCdGetError(). 
Table 2-2 
Value Macro  Result  
-1 SCECdErFAIL Failed to issue sceCdGetError() function  
0x00 SCECdErNO No error  
0x01 SCECdErABRT Abort command received  
0x13 SCECdErNORDY Processing command  
0x14 SCECdErCUD Not appropriate for disc in drive  
0x30 SCECdErREAD Problem occurred during read  
0x31 SCECdErTRMOPN Tray opened during playback  
0xfd SCECdErREADCF Read command issue failed 
Notes 
This function should be called immediately after calling the sceCdMmode() function. 
See also 
sceCdMmode() 
 

===== PAGE 26 =====

2-14    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Library System Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdSetEEReadMode                                              
Set mode for reading data 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.3 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
u_int sceCdSetEEReadMode (  
 u_int mode); Read mode specification 
Initial value is zero. If multiple values are specified, the 
logical OR is taken. 
SCECdNoCheckReady 
   Do not confirm that the drive is ready when a command 
   is issued. 
SCECdNoWriteBackDCache 
   Do not perform WriteBackDCache to EE memory when 
   a command is issued. 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function changes the method by which read commands are issued from the EE to the IOP according 
to the value of the mode argument. 
Although the execution speed of a read operation can be increased by leaving out processing, the result of 
the operation will be indeterminate if there are collisions between commands sent to the drive, and if the 
cache in EE memory becomes incoherent. 
Notes 
Use this function with great care. 
Return value 
Returns the previous setting. 
See also 
sceCdRead(), sceCdReadChain(), and sceCdReadIOPm

 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries

sceCdPOffCallback                                               
Define PlayStation 2 power-off callback function 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.2.2 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
int *sceCdPOffCallback (  
 void (*func)(void *) Address of the callback function 
 void *addr) Address of the callback argument 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
For compatibility with the hard disk drive (EXPANSION BAY type), in order to use the hard disk this function 
must be used to perform hard disk power-off processing. It is only for an EXPANSION BAY type hard disk 
drive. This function should be called after loading hard disk drive-related modules (such as dev9.irx and 
pfs.irx). 
The function sets the callback func that is to be called when the power-off operation is performed. 
When a callback is set, the function func is called when the power-off operation is performed. 
The function func is called by the interrupt handler. 
If 0 is specified for func, no callback will occur. 
When cdvdfsv.irx (cdvd_ee_driver) has been unloaded, use the standard I/O function 
scePowerOffHandler(). 
Return value 
Returns the address of the callback function set previously.  
If the callback has not been set, returns 0.

2-16    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Power Management Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdPowerOff                                                  
PlayStation 2 power OFF 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.2.2 April 28, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdPowerOff (  
 int *stat) Status 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function issues a PlayStation 2 PowerOff request. 
This function must be used in power-off processing when a hard disk drive or network adaptor (smap.irx) is 
used. 
For details about power-off processing, refer to the CD(DVD)-ROM library, PlayStation File System (pfs) and 
network (inet) overviews. 
Notes 
When calling this function, make sure it is executed after an interrupt is detected by sceCdPOffCallback() 
and the hard disk drive is powered off. 
<Sample power-off processing function calling sequence when a hard disk drive is used> 
        printf("power off request has come.\n"); 
        /* close all files */ 
        sceDevctl("pfs:", PDIOC_CLOSEALL, NULL, 0, NULL, 0); 
        /* dev9 power off, need to power off PS2 */ 
        while(sceDevctl("dev9x:", DDIOC_OFF, NULL, 0, NULL, 0) < 0); 
        /* PS2 power off */ 
        while(!sceCdPowerOff(&stat) || stat); 
        while(1); 
Notes: 
• With a hard disk drive (EXPANSION BAY type), if the RESET button on the system unit is pressed 
between the time hard disk power-off processing is performed and PlayStation 2 system unit power-off 
processing is performed, the PlayStation 2 system unit will be reset. 
• When cdvdfsv.irx (cdvd_ee_driver) has been un loaded, use the devctl command CDIOC_POWEROFF. 
Return value 
If there is an error, returns 0. 
stat return value  bit7: 1 Command error

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Time Acquisition Function   2-17 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Time Acquisition Function 
sceCdReadClock                                                   
Get date and time 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.5 April 28, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdReadClock (  
 sceCdCLOCK *rtc) Address of structure where date and time are stored 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
Gets the date and time from the PlayStation 2's built-in real-time clock. 
Notes 
For this function to use a controller which performs drive-related processing, an interval of 300(msec) must 
be cleared when calling it continuously. 
Also, the following values are returned in the stat member of the rtc time storage structure. 
bit 0:  Clock stop detected 
bit 1:  Clock battery monitoring voltage problem 
bit 2:  CTL reg problem 
bit 7:  Command error 
Processing should take into account the case of the internal battery running down. In particular, with 
Release 2.4 and later, when sceCdCLOCK.stat != 0, the default value that the library keeps (00/03/04 
05:06:07) is returned. However, with libraries of earlier releases, the return value is undefined. 
Example: When the battery is exhausted in Release 2.4 and later 
      rtc.stat   = 0x05; 
          rtc.second = 0x07; 
          rtc.minute = 0x06; 
          rtc.hour   = 0x05; 
          rtc.day    = 0x04; 
          rtc.month  = 0x03; 
          rtc.year   = 0x00;       
Furthermore, when the battery is exhausted, if unlimited retries are attempted when sceCdCLOCK.stat != 
0, a hangup will essentially occur. Therefore, be sure to write your code such that it escapes from the retry 

===== PAGE 30 =====

2-18    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Time Acquisition Function    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
loop after a finite number of retries are attempted (include an error check that invokes the 
sceCdReadClock() function while retries are being performed). 
It is permissible to have your code ultimately use the default value. 
Note: The default value is normally updated when the realtime clock is read. However, if reading fails 
(sceCdCLOCK.stat != 0) for some reason (such as if an interval of approx. 300ms does not occur when the 
clock is read continuously), the latest value will be returned. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 1. 
If there is an error, returns 0.

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Callback Setting Functions   2-19 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Callback Setting Functions 
sceCdCallback 
Define sceCdSync callback function 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.3 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdCallback (  
 void (*func)(int)) Address of callback function 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
Sets the callback func called when a non-blocking function terminates. 
When the callback is set, the function func is called when the non-blocking function terminates. 
The function func is called as a callback thread. 
If func is set to 0 or the command fails to issue, the callback does not occur. 
Moreover, a callback cannot be set when a function that has already caused a callback is executing. 
The function code of the cause of the callback is passed to the callback function in the first argument, as 
shown below. 
Table 2-3 
Macro Result 
SCECdFuncRead sceCdRead() function has terminated. 
SCECdFuncReadCDDA sceCDDARead() function has terminated. 
SCECdFuncGetToc sceCdGetToc () function has terminated. 
SCECdFuncSeek  sceCdSeek () function has terminated.  
SCECdFuncStandby sceCdStandby () function has terminated.  
SCECdFuncStop sceCdStop() function has terminated. 
SCECdFuncPause sceCdPause() function has terminated.  
SCECdFuncBreak sceCdBreak() function has terminated. 
 
Note about callback functions 
Calling a function that generates a callback, such as sceCdRead(), while a callback is executing, is not 
supported. 
Return value 
Returns the address of the previously set callback function. Returns 0 if no callback was set.

Description 
Breaks the executing command (e.g., sceCdPause(), sceCdRead(), sceCdSeek(), sceCdStandby(),

Value Macro Result

Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.1 June 13, 2005 
Syntax 
int sceCdDiskReady (  
 int mode) Check mode (0: blocking, 1: non-blocking) 
When mode is set to non-blocking, the operating 
conditions of other threads must be thoroughly 
considered. 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
Checks the drive status and determines if a command can be issued. SCECdNotReady is returned when 
the type of media in the drive is being determined.

2-28    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - CD/DVD Drive Management Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdGetToc 
Read TOC 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.1 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdGetToc (  
 u_char *toc) Address returned by location table information (a 1024 
byte area is required). 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
Gets TOC sector information from CD-ROM. 
The structure of the data that is read is as follows. 
Information is stored at Points A0,A1,A2,00,01,...98,99 in the order shown below. 
Four bytes of 00 data follow the Point information (This data is used to adjust the transfer size.)  
 
 Point A0 (first track number on the disk)  
 Point A1 (last track number on the disk) 
 Point A2 (readout start time) 
 Point 01 (track 01 start time) 
 Point 02 (track 02 start time) 
            :  
 Point 98 (track 98 start time) 
 Point 99 (track 99 start time) 
 00000000 
 
10 bytes of information are stored at each Point for a total of 1020 bytes. 
Including the four bytes of zeroes, a total of 1024 bytes of data are stored. 
The data structure of each Point is as follows.  
addr/ctrl 0th Byte 
Track No. (=00) 1st Byte 
Index No. (Point) 2nd Byte 
Undefined 3rd Byte 
Undefined 4th Byte 
Undefined 5th Byte 
zero = 00h 6th Byte 
absolute Min. 7th Byte 

===== PAGE 41 =====

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - CD/D VD Drive Management Functions   2-29 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
absolute Sec. 8th Byte 
absolute Frame 9th Byte 
 
The meaning of the data is different for each Point, as shown here. 
Table 2-10 
Point Meaning  
Point A0: absolute Min. Firs t track number on the disc 
 absolute Sec. CD-ROM extensions 
  0x10:CD-I 
  0x20:CD-ROM XA 
Point A1: absolute Min. Last  track number on the disc 
Point A2: absolute Min., Sec., Fram e  Readout absolute start time 
(Min., Sec., Frame) 
Point 00-99: absolute Min., Sec., Frame Track number 00-99 absolute 
start time (Min., Sec., Frame)  
 
Notes 
The SUBQs located inside the TOC describe how the data is laid out for the entire disk. What information is 
indicated by a particular SUBQ is determined by that SUBQ's INDEX NO. Note that Points are simply the 
INDEX numbers of SUBQs within the TOC. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 1. 
If there is an error, returns 0.

2-34    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - CD/DVD Drive Management Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdRead 
Read data 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.1 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdRead (  
 u_int lsn, Logical sector number at which to begin reading 
 u_int sectors, Number of sectors to read 
 void *buf, Read buffer 
 sceCdRMode *mode) Read mode sceCdRMode structure address 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
A seek is performed to the starting read position indicated by lsn. 
The number of sectors of data specified by the sectors argument is read from StartPoint on the CD(DVD)-
ROM and placed in the memory specified by buf. The head is then put in the pause state. 
Notes 
CD-DA and DVD-video data cannot be read. 
Since this is a non-blocking function, the actual completion of the data transfer must be detected using 
sceCdSync(). 
Note on using this function  
When data is transferred to the EE, sometimes the library will adjust the alignment of the buffer address. 
Therefore, in the interest of speed, it is best to use 64-byte alignment as much as possible. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 1. 
If there is an error, returns 0.  
After this function is successfully invoked, the following error information can be obtained by first waiting for 
the command to complete using a function such as sceCdSync(), then calling sceCdGetError().

2-36    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - CD/DVD Drive Management Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdReadCDDA 
Read CD-DA data 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.8 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdReadCDDA (   
u_int lsn, Starting logical sector number to read 
u_int sectors,  Number of sectors to read 
void *buf,  Read buffer 
sceCdRMode *mode) Address of read mode structure (sceCdRMode) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
This function seeks to the starting read position indicated by lsn, reads the number of CD-DA sectors 
indicated by sectors from the CD/DVD-ROM starting at that point, and stores the data in the memory area 
specified by buf. Subsequently, the head is placed in a pause state. At the same time, the digital out is de-
muted. 
The sceCdRMode datapattern can have the following value. 
Table 2-15 
Value Macro Meaning 
0 SCECdSecS2352 Transfer size  is fixed at 2352 bytes 
 
The sceCdRMode spindlctrl can have the following value. 
 
Table 2-16 
Value Macro Meaning 
0 SCECdSpinStm Rotate at the recommended speed for the stream 
1 SCECdSpinNom Rotate at the ma ximum speed that the driver can 
read out 
2 SCECdSpinX1 Rotate at the standard speed for a CD-DA read 
Notes 
Because this is a non-blocking function, the actual completion of transfer must be detected using 
sceCdSync(). 
The CD-DA data that is read is not precise because error correction is performed during sound playback. In 
addition, some CCCDs (computer-controlled CDs) may not be readable. 
Precautions when using this function 
When transferring data to the EE, the library may internally adjust the alignment of the buffer address. In the 
interest of transfer speed, it is best to align the buffer on a 64-byte boundary.

 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries

If the function completes successfully, 1 is returned.

2-38    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - CD/DVD Drive Management Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdReadChain               
Batch read data   
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.6 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdReadChain (  
u_int *tag, Pointer to the read parameter storage data sequence 
 The data sequence structure is as follows: 
    tag= { lsn,  sectors,  buf, 
              lsn,  sectors,  buf, 
               :           :          :     Max. 64 sequences 
    0xffffffff, 0xffffffff, 0xffffffff }; 
 0xffffffff is placed at the end of the data sequence 
 u_int lsn:   Logical sector number where reading 
starts 
 u_int sectors:   Number of sectors to read 
 u_int buf: The following kind of value indicating the 
read buffer position 
 bit31..bit2: High-order 30 bits of the read buffer 
address 
 bit0:  0: EE memory  
1: IOP memory 
 sceCdRMode *mode) Read mode sceCdRMode structure address 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
Reads at most 64 sets of data in a batch according to the contents of the read parameter storage data 
sequence. CD-DA data and DVD-video data cannot be read.  
When EE memory is specified as the read buffer, the addresses must adhere to 64-byte alignment.  
Since this is a non-blocking function, the sceCdSync() function must be used to detect the end of the 
actual transfer. 
Return Value 
If successful, returns 1. 
If there is an error, returns 0.  
After this function is successfully invoked, the following error information can be obtained by first waiting for 
the command to complete using a function such as sceCdSync(), then calling sceCdGetError().

sceCdReadIOPm 
Read data to IOP memory        
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.4 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCdReadIOPm (  
 u_int lsn, Logical sector number at which to begin reading 
 u_int sectors, Number of sectors to read 
 void *buf, Read buffer (IOP memory) 
 sceCdRMode *mode) Read mode sceCdRMode structure address 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function seeks to the read start position indicated by lsn, reads the specified number of sectors from 
the CD(DVD)-ROM's StartPoint, and fills IOP memory starting at buf. Then the head is placed in pause 
state. 
Notes

This is a non-blocking function, so it is necessary to detect the actual end of transfer using sceCdSync(). 
Return value 
If successful, returns 1. 
If there is an error, returns 0.  
After this function is successfully invoked, the following error information can be obtained by first waiting for 
the command to complete using a function such as sceCdSync(), then calling sceCdGetError().

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - CD/D VD Drive Management Functions   2-47 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdSync 
Wait for command completion 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.1 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceCdSync (  
 int mode) 0x00: Wait for completion of command  (blocking) 
0x01: Check current status and return immediately  
         (non-blocking). 
         When using this mode, the operating conditions of 
         other threads must be thoroughly considered. 
Calling conditions 
Can be called in an interrupt disabled state (blocking type cannot be called in interrupt-disabled state) 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
When mode is set to 0x00, this function waits for the command being executed to complete and returns 0. 
When mode is set to 0x01, this function checks the execution state of the command and returns either 0 or 
1. 
When this function is used for polling in non-blocking mode in a multithreaded environment, a function such 
as DelayThread() must be used so that there is sufficient room for other threads to operate. 
Return value 
Table 2-26 
Value Result 
0 Completed 
1 Not completed 
See also 
sceCdRead(), sceCdSeek(), sceCdStop(), sceCdStandby(), sceCdPause()

sceCdTrayReq 
Open and close the tray 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.3 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceCdTrayReq(  
 int mode, Tray control mode 
SCECdTrayOpen: Open tray 
SCECdTrayClose: Close tray 
SCECdTrayCheck: Get tray state change 
 u_int *traycnt) Address for returning whether or not there was a tray 
state change 
0:  Tray was not opened. 
1:  Tray was opened.

Can be called from a thread

Return value

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - CD/D VD Drive Management Functions   2-49 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
File Control Functions 
File control functions 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.4 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
The following file control functions are supported.  
#include <sifdev.h> Refer to the documentation on the standard I/O functions for arguments. 
sceClose()  
sceDclose()  
sceDevctl()  
sceDopen()  
sceDread()  
sceIoctl2()  
sceLseek()  
sceOpen() Additional arguments for the sceOpen() function: 
filename   cdrom0: + filename (ISO9660 Level 1) 
flags        Access mode. Specify either of the following constants. 
                 SCE_RDONLY Open only for reading 
                 SCE_CdSTREAM Open only for reading a stream 
                 SCE_CdCACHE  When reading a file, perform a cache 
operation to the device specified by the 
devctl() function command 
CDIOC_FSCACHEINIT. (A product can only 
use this access mode for a file whose type 
is defined by the TRC as installable on the 
hard disk drive.) 
sceRead()  
Description 
These functions support I/O operations on individual files. 
Precaution when using the sceLseek() function 
When using lseek() or sceLseek() with the cdrom: device, the sign of the second argument should be 
reversed relative to its normal meaning, but only when SCE_SEEK_END is specified for the third argument. 
For example, if you wanted to move the file pointer to a position one byte from the end of the file, you 
would normally specify the following. 
 
sceLseek(fd, -1, SEEK_END) 
 
However, for the cdrom: device, the correct specification should be: 
 
sceLseek(fd, 1, SEEK_END) 
 
Precautions when a file is opened exclusively for stream reading with SCE_CdSTREAM 
1. The size argument of the sceRead() function must be a multiple of 2048. 
2. The CDIOC_GETERROR command must be used to get read error information. 

===== PAGE 62 =====

2-50    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - CD/DVD Drive Management Functions    
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
3. Once the file has been opened, data is read recu rsively in the background from the CD(DVD)-ROM to 
the streaming buffer. Therefore, a file handling function or a function such as sceCdRead() cannot be 
used to read from the CD(DVD)-ROM until the sceClose() function is called to end streaming.

2-56    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Stream Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCddaStStart 
Start CD-DA streaming 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceCddaStStart(  
u_int lsn, Stream start position (specified as a logical sector 
number) 
sceCdRMode *mode) Address of read mode structure (sceCdRMode) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
This function reads data into the stream buffer starting from the stream's starting position. 
When streaming begins, data is read recursively in the background from the CD/DVD-ROM to the 
streaming buffer. Therefore, you will not be able to call any functions that operate on files or use a function 
like sceCddaRead() to read from the CD(DVD)-ROM, until you call sceCddaStStop() to terminate streaming. 
The only value that can be set for the datapattern in mode is SCECdSecS2352. 
Valid values for spindlctrl in mode are SCECdSpinStm (use recommended stream speed) or 
SCECdSpinX1 (use standard speed). 
Notes 
Starting with Library Release 2.7, processing for the streaming function group was split off into the 
cdvdstm.irx module. 
To perform streaming, a function such as sceSifLoadModule() must be used to load the cdvdstm.irx 
module into the IOP. 
Return value 
When the function completes successfully, 1 is returned. 
If an error occurs, 0 is returned. 
See also 
sceCddaStInit(),sceCddaStRead(),sceCddaStSeek(),sceCddaStSeekF(), 
sceCddaStStat(),sceCddaStStop()

2-66    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - Stream Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceCdStStart 
Start streaming 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 1.4 December 6, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceCdStStart(  
 u_int lsn, Stream start position (specified using logical sector 
number) 
 sceCdRMode *mode) Read mode sceCdRMode structure address 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
Starts reading from the specified stream start position into the stream buffer. 
After streaming starts, data is read from the CD(DVD) into the streaming buffer recurrently in the 
background. This means that functions like the file control functions and sceCDRead() cannot be used to 
read from the CD(DVD)-ROM until streaming has been stopped with sceCdStStop(). 
The only value that can be specified for datapattern mode is SCECdSecS2048.  
For spindlctrl mode use SCECdSpinStm (recommended velocity for streaming).  
CD-DA data and DVD-video data cannot be read. 
Notes 
Starting with Library Release 2.7, processing for the streaming function group was split off into the 
cdvdstm.irx module. 
To perform streaming, a function such as sceSifLoadModule() must be used to load the cdvdstm.irx 
module into the IOP. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 1. 
If there is an error, returns 0. 
See also 
sceCdStInit(), sceCdStRead(), sceCdStSeek(), sceCdStSeekF(), sceCdStStat(), sceCdStStop(), 
sceCdStPause(), sceCdStResume()

2-70    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - devctl Commands 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
CDIOC_DISKRDY                                            
Check drive state 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.3.4 March 29, 2002 
Arguments 
arg  Check mode (0: Blocking, 1: Non-blocking) storage address 
arglen  sizeof(int) 
bufp  Drive state storage address 
buflen  sizeof(int) 
Description 
The CDIOC_DISKRDY command checks the following drive states to determine whether or not a 
command can be issued. 
SCECdComplete is the drive state that allows commands to be issued, and SCECdNotReady is the state 
in which the drive cannot accept commands. The state becomes SCECdNotReady when there is no media 
in the drive. 
For the blocking case, if the drive rotation is unstable, the function will wait until the rotation becomes stable 
before returning. For the non-blocking case, the function will return immediately after checking the status. 
When this function is used for non-blocking polling in a multithread environment, the DelayThread() (or 
equivalent) function must be used to make sure there is room for other threads to run. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 0.  
If there is an error, one of the following is returned. 
Table 2-30 
Value Macro Result 
-11 -EAGAIN Cannot alloca te thread or memory 
-19 -ENODEV Specified device does not exist 
-24 -EMFILE Reached maximu m number of descriptors 
which can be opened 
-48 -EUNSUP Process not supported by device

2-72    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - devctl Commands 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
CDIOC_FSCACHEINIT                                           
Initialize cache system 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.7.1 April 28, 2003 
Arguments 
arg  arg  string for initializing cache system 
arglen  Size of arg string for initializing cache system 
bufp  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen  Reserved. Specify 0. 
Description 
This function initializes the cache system. 
Definition of the arg string used to initialize the cache system 
The following three components separated by commas should be specified for the arg string in order to 
initialize the cache system. 
1. Path where the cach e data will be stored 
device[0-9]:directory 
2.  Number of metadata blocks to allocate when the cache is opened 
(Allocation in IOP memory:  1 block = 2048 (bytes) 
At least one block is required. Up to 128MB can be managed in RAM with one block.) 
3.  Maximum number of blocks in cache area for caching a directory 
(Allocation on cache device: 1 block = 2048 (bytes) 
At least one block is required. One directory can be managed with one block.) 
Example:  "pfs0:/,1,8" 
Since one file descriptor on the caching device is used whenever a cached file is opened, a total of two file 
descriptors are needed. 
Notes 
The cache should be reinitialized after a CDIOC_FSCACHEDELETE is performed. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 0.  
If there is an error, one of the following is returned.

2-76    CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - devctl Commands 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
CDIOC_GETTOC  
Read TOC 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.3.4 July 14, 2003 
Arguments 
arg  Reserved. Specify NULL.  
arglen  Reserved. Specify 0. 
bufp  TOC storage address. 1024-byte area is required 
buflen  1024 
Description 
This command gets the TOC sector information of the CD-ROM. 
The structure of the data that is read is as follows. 
Information is stored at Points A0,A1,A2,00,01,...98,99 in the order shown below. 
Four bytes of 00 data follow the Point information (This data is used to adjust the transfer size.)  
 
 Point A0 (first track number on the disk)  
 Point A1 (last track number on the disk) 
 Point A2 (readout start time) 
 Point 01 (track 01 start time) 
 Point 02 (track 02 start time) 
            :  
 Point 98 (track 98 start time) 
 Point 99 (track 99 start time) 
 00000000 
 
10 bytes of information are stored at each Point for a total of 1020 bytes. 
Including the four bytes of zeroes, a total of 1024 bytes of data are stored. 
The data structure of each Point is as follows.  
addr/ctrl 0th Byte 
Track No. (=00) 1st Byte 
Index No. (Point) 2nd Byte 
Undefined 3rd Byte 
Undefined 4th Byte 
Undefined 5th Byte 
zero = 00h 6th Byte 
absolute Min. 7th Byte 
absolute Sec. 8th Byte 
absolute Frame 9th Byte 
 
The meaning of the data is different for each Point, as shown here. 

===== PAGE 89 =====

 CD(DVD)-ROM Library (for EE) - devctl Commands    2-77 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Table 2-37 
Point Meaning  
Point A0: absolute Min. Firs t track number on the disc 
 absolute Sec. CD-ROM extensions 
  0x10:CD-I 
  0x20:CD-ROM XA 
Point A1: absolute Min. Last  track number on the disc 
Point A2: absolute Min., Sec., Fram e  Readout absolute start time 
(Min., Sec., Frame) 
Point 00-99: absolute Min., Sec., Frame Track number 00-99 absolute 
start time (Min., Sec., Frame)  
 
Notes 
The SUBQs located inside the TOC describe how the data is laid out for the entire disk. What information is 
indicated by a particular SUBQ is determined by that SUBQ's INDEX NO. Note that Points are simply the 
INDEX numbers of SUBQs within the TOC. 
Return value 
If successful, returns 0.  
If there is an error, one of the following is returned. 
Table 2-38 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO Input/Output error 
-11 -EAGAIN Cannot alloca te thread or memory 
-19 -ENODEV Specified device does not exist 
-24 -EMFILE Reached maximu m number of descriptors 
which can be opened 
-48 -EUNSUP Process not supported by device

CDIOC_SPINNOM 
Set adaptive speed control for the standard I/O media spin rate 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libcdvd 2.3.4 March 29, 2002 
Arguments 
arg Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen Reserved. Specify 0. 
bufp Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen  Reserved. Specify 0. 
Description 
This command sets adaptive speed control for the standard I/O media spin rate. This causes data reading 
to begin at the highest spin rate, and when a read error occurs, it lowers the spin rate until reading can be 
performed properly. 
The initial value for the standard I/O spin rate is adaptive speed control.

which can be opened

 dev9 Driver Module - Function    3-3 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Function 
sceDevctl                                                      
Special operations on device 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
dev9 2.3 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceDevctl(  
 const char *name, Device name (dev9x:). 
 int cmd, Operation command. Specify one of the following 
constants. 
    DDIOC_MODEL 
    DDIOC_OFF 
 void *arg, Argument assigned to command. Depends on cmd. 
 unsigned int arglen, arg size 
 void *bufp, Arguments received from command. Depends on 
cmd. 
 unsigned int buflen) bufp size 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
This function performs special operations on a device. For descriptions of each cmd, see the "devctl 
Command List." The device name is dev9x, not dev9. Be careful not to use the wrong name. 
        Example:        sceDevctl ("dev9x:",DDIOC_OFF,  NULL, 0, NULL, 0); 
Return value 
When processing succeeds, a cmd-dependent value is returned. 
When an error occurs, each command returns the following common errors: 
Table 3-1 
Value Macro Result 
-19 ENODEV  The specified device does not exist 
-24 EMFILE The maximum number of descriptors that 
can be opened was reached 
 
Error values can be converted to sceerrno.h error codes by using the sceStdioConvertError() function. The 
low-order 16 bits of the converted error code have the following meanings. 

===== PAGE 110 =====

3-4    dev9 Driver Module - Function     
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Table 3-2 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-ENODEV SCE_ENODEV 0x0013 
-EMFILE SCE_EMFILE 0x0018

 dev9 Driver Module - devctl Commands    3-5 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
devctl Commands 
DDIOC_MODEL                                               
Determine device type 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
dev9 2.3 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
arg  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen arg  size. 
bufp Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen bufp  size. 
Description 
This command determines whether the device is a PC Card type or EXPANSION BAY type device. 
Normally, the application need not perform this operation. 
Return value 
When the device is a network adapter (PC CARD type), 0 is returned. When the device is a hard disk drive 
(EXPANSION BAY type), 1 is returned.         
 

===== PAGE 112 =====

3-6    dev9 Driver Module - devctl Commands 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
DDIOC_OFF                                                 
Power off device 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
dev9 2.3 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
arg  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen arg  size. 
bufp  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen bufp  size. 
Description 
This command powers off the entire dev9 device.  
Through Release 2.5.2, different power off commands were used depending on whether a hard disk drive 
or a network adapter was connected. However, with Release 2.5.3, the functions were unified in dev9 so 
the DDIOC_OFF command should be used for both hard disk drive and network adapter. 
The HDIOC_DEV9OFF command is also retained for compatibility. 
For more information, please refer to the network library overview (inet) and the "Power-off processing" 
section of the PlayStation File System overview (pfs). 
If you issue LoadExecPS2() from a program that uses a dev9 device, turn the power off to the dev9 device 
with the DDIOC_OFF command before calling the function. 
Return value 
When processing succeeds, 0 is returned.

sce_stat                                                       
Partition status 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.2.2 October 11, 2002 
Structure 
struct sce_stat {  
     unsigned int st_mode; Filesystem type of the partition 
SCE_FSTYPE_EMPTY     Empty partition 
SCE_FSTYPE_PFS          pfs 
     unsigned int st_attr; bit 0  Sub-partition 
     unsigned int st_size; Number of sectors in the partition 
     unsigned char st_ctime[8]; Creation time of the partition 
     unsigned char st_atime[8]; byte 0 reserved 
     unsigned char st_mtime[8]; byte 1  Seconds 
byte 2  Minutes 
byte 3  Hours 
byte 4  Day 
byte 5  Month 
byte 6-7 Year (4 digits) 
     unsigned int st_hisize;  
     unsigned int st_private[6] }; word 0    
For the main partition, represents the number of sub-
partitions. For a sub-partition, represents the sub-partition 
number starting from 1. 
Description 
This structure stores partition status. 
See also 
sce_dirent

sceClose                                                              
Close main partition 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceClose(  
 int fd) Previously open file descriptor 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Closes the opened partition and frees the file descriptor. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0. 
If there is an error, the following is returned: 
Table 4-1 
Value Macro Result 
-9 -EBADF fd is not a valid open descriptor. 
-11 -EAGAIN IOP RPC failed.  
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free IOP memory.

low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following values. 
Table 4-2 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-EBADF SCE_EBADF 0x0009 
-EAGAIN SCE_EAGAIN 0x000b 
-ENOMEM SCE_ENOMEM 0x000c 
See also 
sceStdioConvertError()

 Hard Disk Library (for EE) - Functions    4-19 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceOpen                                                                
Create, open main partition 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceOpen(  
 const char *name, Partition identifier string. 
 int flags) Access mode.  
Any of the following constants can be specified. 
A logical OR is performed if more than one is specified. 
  SCE_RDONLY Open as read-only. 
  SCE_RDWR Open as read/write. 
  SCE_CREAT Create a new partition if one does not  
                          exist. 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Creates, opens the main partition. Assigns a file descriptor to the file that has been opened. Certain 
partitions cannot be opened simultaneously. The partition identifier string consists of device name + unit 
number + ':'+ a string made from a sequence of the following strings separated by commas. 
Table 4-19 
String Description 
Partition ID This is a unique ID fo r the entire disk and is formally 
issued by SCE, however, any character string can be 
used as long as it is unique during the creation stage 
and is no more than 32 characters long. 
Full password The password required for read/write access. The 
password can be up to 8 characters long. 
Read-only password The password for read-only access. The password can 
be up to 8 characters long. 
Partition size Character st ring which specifies the size of the partition. 
The valid characters strings are shown below: 
128M, 256M, 512M, 1G, 2G, 4G, 8G, 16G, 32G 
Filesystem name At present, only "PFS" is valid. 
     
All of these need to be specified for creation, except for the passwords. To open a partition, specify up to 
the required password. 
Example 1:  Creation with password specifications 
 sceOpen(“hdd0:BISLPS-XXXXX,fpwd,rpw d,128M,PFS”,SCE_CREAT|SCE_RDWR); 

===== PAGE 134 =====

4-20    Hard Disk Library (for EE) - Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Example 2:  Creation without password specifications 
 sceOpen(“hdd0:BISLPS-XXXXX,,,128 M,PFS”, SCE_CREAT|SCE_RDWR); 
Example 3:  Open with a password  
 sceOpen(“hdd0:BISLPS-X XXXX,fpwd”, SCE_RDWR); 
Example 4:  Open without a password  
 sceOpen(“hdd0:BISLPS-XXXXX”, SCE_RDWR); 
Example 5:  Open with a read-only password 
 sceOpen(“hdd0:BISLPS-XXXXX,,rpwd”, SCE_RDONLY); 
Notes 
If an opened partition is not closed before the filesystem driver performs a format or mount of the partition, 
then an EBUSY error is returned. 
Return value 
If termination is normal, returns the file descriptor (value 0 or more). 
If there is an error, returns one of the following: 
Table 4-20 
Value Macro Result 
-2 -ENOENT    Specified partition does not exist. 
-5 -EIO       I/O error. 
-11 -EAGAIN IOP RPC failed.  
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free IOP memory. 
-13 -EACCES No access rights. 
-16 -EBUSY     The specified partition is already 
open. 
-19 -ENODEV    Specified device does not exist. 
-22 -EINVAL    Invalid arguments were specified.  
-24 -EMFILE    Reached maximum number of 
descriptors that can be opened.  
-28 -ENOSPC    No free space. 
-65540 -SCE_EVERSIONMISS Incompatib le with version of fileio

Library Introduced Documentation last modified

Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceRead(  
 int fd, File descriptor of the read target 
 void *buf, Address of the buffer that will store the read data

Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state)

4-28    Hard Disk Library (for EE) - devctl Commands 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
devctl Commands  
HDIOC_DEV9OFF  
Power OFF device 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Arguments 
arg Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen  Size of arg. 
bufp  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen Size of bufp. 
Description 
Powers off the entire dev9 device to which the hard disk drive is connected.  
This processing should be performed before powering off the main unit.  
Note:  When this processing is performed, other devices connected to dev9 (the network adapter) are also 
powered off. 
The specifications for dev9 device power off processing were changed beginning with Release 2.5.3, so 
the dev9 command DDIOC_OFF should be used even when a hard disk drive is connected. 
The HDIOC_DEV9OFF command has also been retained for compatibility. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0.

 Hard Disk Library (for EE) - devctl Commands    4-31 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
HDIOC_FREESECTOR                                          
Get installable size 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.3.1 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
arg Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen  Size of arg. 
bufp Pointer to an unsigned 32-bit integer, for storing 
installable size, in sectors. 
buflen  Size of bufp. 
Description 
Returns the installable size in bufp. The installable size will be equal to the disk's free space as indicated in 
the browser. All free space over 1 GB is added, and for free space less than 1 GB (512M, 256M and 128M) 
an addition is performed for up to one area, respectively. If there is more than one free area less than 1 GB, 
those areas will not be added as such, but as aggregates of smaller areas. However, in this case, any area 
that has been counted once will not be counted again. 
For example, assume there are two areas of 512 MB and one of 128 MB. In this case, the first 512 MB 
area is simply counted; the second 512 MB area is counted as areas of 256 MB and 128 MB. Upon finding 
the next 128 MB area, since 128 MB has already been counted once, that area is not counted -it is 
ignored. 
Note: As shown in the example above, the return value for this devctl command does not simply give the 
amount of free space. Rather, it may return a value that is smaller than the actual partition size that can be 
created. To find the actual partition size that can be created, the partition list and the capacity of the entire 
disk would need to be obtained. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0. 
If there is an error, the following is returned: 
Table 4-30 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
 
The error value can be converted to an sceerrno.h error code by using the sceStdioConvertError() function. 
The low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following value. 
Table 4-31 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-EIO SCE_EIO 0x0005 
See also 
sceStdioConvertError() 
 

===== PAGE 146 =====

4-32    Hard Disk Library (for EE) - devctl Commands 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
HDIOC_IDLE                                                
Set idle mode 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
arg Pointer to an 8-bit variable that stores the setting value. 
arglen  Size of arg. 
bufp  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen  Size of bufp. 
Description 
Sets the amount of time after which the hard disk drive will transition to idle mode.  
The default time for the hdd module to transition to idle mode is 21 minutes and 15 seconds. The settable 
values are shown below.  
Table 4-32 
Value Meaning 
0x00         timeout disable 
0x01 - 0xf0  (value * 5) s 
0xf1 - 0xfb  ((value - 240) * 30) min 
0xfc         21 min 
0xfd         Period between 8 and 12 hours 
0xfe         Reserved 
0xff         21 min 15 s 
 
Example: 
u_char standbytimer = 0xff; 
devctl("hdd0:", HDIOC_IDLE, &standbytimer, sizeof(char), NULL, 0); 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0. 
If there is an error, the following is returned: 
Table 4-33 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
 
The error value can be converted to an sceerrno.h error code by using the sceStdioConvertError() function. 
The low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following value. 
Table 4-34 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-EIO SCE_EIO 0x0005

 Hard Disk Library (for EE) - devctl Commands    4-35 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
HDIOC_MAXSECTOR                                           
Get maximum size of partition that can be created 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.2.2 July 26, 2004 
Syntax 
arg  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen  Size of arg. 
bufp Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen  Size of bufp. 
Description 
Gets the maximum size of a partition that can be created (in units of sectors). 
The maximum size of a partition that can be created is calculated so as not to exceed 1/32 of the capacity 
of the entire hard disk. As a result, a value that is outside the range of valid partition sizes that can be 
created which the system permits (2
n
 bytes in the range 128MB to 32GB), may be returned. 
Therefore, the size of the partition to be created must be checked separately. 
One sector is equal to 512 bytes. 
Return value 
Returns a value that is a power of 2 (2 ^ n) as a signed 32-bit integer.

4-38    Hard Disk Library (for EE) - devctl Commands 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
HDIOC_SWAPTMP                                             
Exchange partition information with _tmp 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
arg  Partition identifer string. 
arglen  Size of arg. 
bufp  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen  Size of bufp. 
Description 
Exchanges partition information with the _tmp partition. 
Creates a new partition and copies the contents of the existing partition, then deletes the copy source.  
Can be used as a substitute for defrag, etc. Processing is similar to that of the filesystem rename(), etc., 
however, this command uses _tmp instead of creating a partition with a new partition ID. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0. 
If there is an error, the following is returned: 
Table 4-40 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free memory. 
-13 -EACCES No access rights. 
-22 -EINVAL Invalid arguments were specified. 
  
Error values can be converted to sceerrno.h error codes by using the sceStdioConvertError() function. The 
low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following values. 
Table 4-41 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-EIO SCE_EIO 0x0005 
-ENOMEM SCE_ENOMEM 0x000c 
-EACCES SCE_EACCES 0x000d 
-EINVAL SCE_EINVAL 0x0016 
See also 
sceStdioConvertError()

HIOCDELSUB                                               
Delete sub-partition 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
hdd 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
arg Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen Size of arg. 
bufp Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen  Size of bufp. 
Description 
Deletes a sub-partition. The sub-partition that was added last is deleted. If a filesystem has already been 
created in this partition and if a sub-partition is deleted without first reducing the size of the filesystem, then 
the filesystem will be destroyed. Usually, the application is not required to perform this operation directly.  
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0. 
If there is an error, returns one of the following: 
Table 4-44 
Value Macro Result 
-2 -ENOENT Subpartition to be deleted does not exist. 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free memory. 
-13 -EACCES No access rights. 
 
Error values can be converted to sceerrno.h error codes by using the sceStdioConvertError() function. The 
low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following values. 
Table 4-45 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-ENOENT SCE_ENOENT 0x0002 
-EIO SCE_EIO 0x0005 
-ENOMEM SCE_ENOMEM 0x000c 
-EACCES SCE_EACCES 0x000d 
See also 
sceStdioConvertError()

Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 April 16, 2001 
Structure 
struct sce_stat {  
 unsigned int st_mode; File mode 
    bit 0 Execute permission (other) 
    bit 1 Write permission (other) 
    bit 2 Read permission (other) 
    bit 3 Execute permission (group) 
    bit 4 Write permission (group) 
    bit 5 Read permission (group) 
    bit 6 Execute permission (user) 
    bit 7 Write permission (user) 
    bit 8 Read permission (user) 
    bit 9 Reserved 
    bit10 Reserved 
    bit11 Reserved 
    bit12-15 File type 
                    1 Directory 
                    2 Normal file  
                    4 Symbolic link  
  unsigned int st_attr; Flag compatible with memory card mode 
  unsigned int st_size; File size (64 bit) 
  unsigned char st_ctime[8]; Creation time 
  unsigned char st_atime[8]; This field is updated at the same time as last access time 
and last update time. 
  unsigned char st_mtime[8]; Last update time 
   byte0      Reserved 
   byte1      Seconds 
   byte2      Minutes 
   byte3      Hours 
   byte4      Day 
   byte5      Month 
   byte6-7   (4 digits) 
  unsigned int st_hisize;  
  unsigned int st_private[6]}; word0 uid 
word1 gid 
word2 Number of zones used by the file

sceDopen                                                           
Open a directory 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceDopen(  
 const char *name) Directory path name(including device name + ':') 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Opens a directory. Assigns a file descriptor to the open directory. Directory path name is "pfs" + unit

 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceFormat                                                          
Format filesystem 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceFormat(  
 const char *devname, Filesystem device name (pfs:) 
 const char *blockdevname, Block device name of partition created in advance.  
    (Example: 'hdd0:BISLPS-XXXXXX,fpwd") 
 void *arg, Pointer to zone size variable and fragment option. 
 int arglen) Size of arg. 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Builds a new filesystem. The specified zone size must be a power of 2 (2 ^ n) and in the range 2K - 128K. 
Efficiency will be improved if the zone size is set to a smaller value if most of the files to be created are 
small, and to a larger value if most of the files to be created are large.  
Example: 
        int zonesz = 8192; 
        sceFormat("pfs:", "hdd0:BISLPS-XXXXXX,fpwd", &zonesz, sizeof(int)); 
 
In addition, formatting can also be performed if fragmentation was intentionally done for verification 
purposes during development. 
 int arg[3]; 
        arg[0] = 8192;              // zone size 
        arg[1] = 0x00002d66;        // -f 
        arg[2] = 0x01030f0f;        // fragment bit pattern 
        sceFormat("pfs:", "hdd0:test", &arg, sizeof(arg)); 
         
Each bit of the bit pattern corresponds to a zone. For example, if 0x0f0f0f0f is specified, formatting will be 
performed with a repeated pattern in which four zones are used and four zones are empty. 
Notes 
Note that when this operation is performed, a filesystem previously created on the specified partition will be 
initialized. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0. 
If there is an error, returns one of the following:

-2 -ENOENT Specified partition does not exist.

 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMount                                                           
Mount device 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceMount(  
 const char *fsname, Character string which specifies filesystem device name 
and unit number after mounting. 
 const char *devname, Character string which identifies the required device that 
will be used to open the block device to be mounted. 
 int flag, Mount flag. Any of the following constants can be 
specified. 
For multiple specifications, take the logical OR. 
     SCE_MT_RDWR             Mount as read/write enabled. 
     SCE_MT_RDONLY          Mount as read-only.  
     SCE_MT_ROBUST Mount in ROBUST node.  
     SCE_MT_ERRCHECK   Set an error if there is anything
                                           abnormal in the filesystem  
                                           when mounting. 
 void *arg, Reserved 
 int arglen) Size of arg 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Mounts the block device specified by devname on the filesystem logical device specified by fsname.  
devname usually specifies a string that identifies a previously created partition. If the mount is done read-
only, providing only a read-only password is acceptable, but to allow mounting for read/write, a full 
password is required.  
When SCE_MT_ROBUST is specified, filesystem information will always be updated. When any operation 
that causes a change to the filesystem (such as sceMkdir(), sceWrite()) is performed, it is immediately 
synchronized with the disk. Furthermore, updating of the close completion flag for memory card 
compatibility is only performed in ROBUST mode.  
When SCE_MT_ERRCHECK is specified and an abnormality occurs in the filesystem, an EIO error will be 
returned. When an abnormality is seen in the filesystem, a prompt filesystem check is recommended. Even 
with an abnormality in the filesystem, the trouble-free portion of the filesystem is still readable, provided that 
the filesystem is not updated. However, writing should not be performed because it may worsen the 
problem.  
Examples: 
sceMount("pfs0:", "hdd0:tst1,fpwd1", SCE_MT_RDWR, NULL, 0); 
sceMount("pfs1:", "hdd0:tst2,fpwd2", SCE_MT_RDWR|SCE_MT_ROBUST, NULL, 0);

5-34    PlayStation File System (for EE) - Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceRead                                                            
Read file 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceRead(  
 int fd, File descriptor of the read target 
 void *buf, Address of the buffer that will store the read data 
 int count) Read data size 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Reads a maximum of count bytes from the file that was previously opened, into the buffer starting from the 
address specified by buf.  
Performance is lowered if the buffer is not 64-byte aligned, so 64-byte alignment is recommended. 
Performance will improve if count is a multiple of 512. It is recommended that a multiple of 512 be used as 
much as possible. Even if reading in 512-byte units is not possible, reads should be performed in at least 4-
byte units (64-byte units if possible). To the degree that transfers are performed once in large units, 
performance will improve even more.   
If an EIO | 0x10000 error occurs, either overwrite the file or delete it completely without performing a 
filesystem check. 
Notes 
SPR cannot be specified to buf. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns the number of bytes read. The file position is advanced by this amount 
only. A return value of 0 means end of file.  
If there was an error, one of the following is returned: 
Table 5-31 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
-9 -EBADF fd is not a valid open descriptor. 
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free memory. 
-22 -EINVAL An invalid argument was specified. 
-65541 -EIO | 0x10000 Bad sector was found while reading 
the file contents. 

===== PAGE 195 =====

 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries

low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following values. 
Table 5-32 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-EIO SCE_EIO 0x0005 
-EBADF SCE_EBADF 0x0009

5-40    PlayStation File System (for EE) - Functions

sceRename                                                          
Change file/directory name 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceRename(  
 const char *oldname,  Name of file/directory before change 
 const char *newname) Name of file/directory after change 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Renames file. If required, performs movement between directories. If newname already exists, it is 
automatically replaced if the following conditions are met.  
• oldname is a file and newname is also a file. 
• oldname is a directory and newname is also a directory. 
• newname is a directory and it is empty. 
• newname is not open. 
If newname exists, it is guaranteed that the original newname will remain unchanged even if the operation 
fails for any reason.  
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0.  
If there is an error, returns one of the following: 
Table 5-37 
Value Macro Result 
-2 -ENOENT Specified file does not exist. 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free memory. 
-13 -EACCES No access permission. 
-16 -EBUSY The file is open or it is a working 
directory. 
-19 -ENODEV Specified device does not exist. 
-20 -ENOTDIR oldname is directory but newname 
is not a directory. 
-21 -EISDIR oldname is a file and newname is a 
directory. 

===== PAGE 201 =====

 PlayStation File System (for EE) - Functions    5-41 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Value Macro Result 
-22 -EINVAL "." or ".." was specified, or 
newname includes part of the path 
of oldname. In other words, tried to 
change a directory into its own 
subdirectory. 
-24 -EMFILE Reached the maximum number of 
descriptors that can be opened. 
-28 -ENOSPC No free space. 
-30 -EROFS Write access was requested for a 
file from a read-only filesystem. 
-90 -ENOTEMPTY newname is a directory but the 
directory is not empty. 
-91 -ENAMETOOLONG  File path name is too long. 
-92 -ELOOP Too many symbolic links 
encountered when resolving the 
path name.

sceSync                                                            
Synchronize buffer cache and disk 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceSync(  
 const char *name, Device name 
 int flag) Flag (Reserved, not used) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state)         
Description 
To avoid reading and writing to a slow disk, the filesystem keeps data in memory. This function flushes the 
contents of the filesystem's buffer cache in this memory to the disk. Flushing also includes the cache on 
the disk as well. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0.  
If there is an error, returns one of the following: 
Table 5-43 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
-19 -ENODEV Specified device does not exist.

low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following values.

sceStdioConvertError()
