5-48    PlayStation File System (for EE) - Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceWrite                                                           
Write to file 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
#include <sifdev.h>  
int sceWrite(  
 int fd, File descriptor of the file to be written. 
 const void *buf, Address that stores the data to be written. 
 int count) Size of the data to be written. 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Writes a maximum of count bytes from the buffer indicated by buf into the file referenced by the file 
descriptor fd. Performance is lowered if the buffer is not 64-byte aligned, so 64-byte alignment is 
recommended. Performance will improve if count is a multiple of 512. It is recommended that a multiple of 
512 be used as much as possible. Even if reading in 512-byte units is not possible, reads should be 
performed in at least 4-byte units (64-byte units if possible). To the degree that transfers are performed 
once in large units, performance will improve even more.   
If an EIO|0x10000 error occurs, delete the file without performing a filesystem check. 
Notes 
SPR cannot be specified to buf. 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns the number of bytes written. The file position is advanced by this 
amount only. 
If there is an error, returns one of the following: 
Table 5-47 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
-9 -EBADF fd is not a valid open descriptor. 
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free memory. 
-22 -EINVAL Invalid argument was specified.  
-28 -ENOSPC No free space. 
-65541 -EIO  |0x10000 Bad sector was found while writing 
the file contents. 
 
Error values can be converted to sceerrno.h error codes by using the sceStdioConvertError() function. The 
low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following values. 

===== PAGE 209 =====

 PlayStation File System (for EE) - Functions    5-49 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Table 5-48 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-EIO SCE_EIO 0x0005 
-EBADF SCE_EBADF 0x0009 
-ENOMEM SCE_ENOMEM 0x000c 
-EINVAL SCE_EINVAL 0x0016 
-ENOSPC SCE_ENOSPC 0x001c 
-EIO|0x10000 SCE_EDEVICE_BROKEN 0x9001 
See also 
sceStdioConvertError()

5-52    PlayStation File System (for EE) - devctl Commands

Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.3 March 29, 2002 
Arguments 
arg Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen Size of arg. 
bufp  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen  Size of bufp. 
Description

 PlayStation File System (for EE) - ioctl2 Commands    5-55 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
ioctl2 Commands 
PIOCALLOC                                                 
Allocate area 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Arguments 
arg Number of allocated zones. 
arglen Size of arg. 
bufp  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
buflen  Size of bufp. 
Description 
Allocates an area that can be used by a file. In pfs, the speed of allocating an area for a new file is not very 
high. When the file size is approximately known and a large amount of data is to be continuously written to 
a file, write performance will be improved if the area is allocated in advance before writing. 
Example: 
u_int size = 1024*1024; 
sceIoctl2(fd, PIOCALLOC, &size, sizeof(int), NULL, 0); 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns 0.  
If there is an error, returns one of the following: 
Table 5-49 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free memory. 
-13 -EACCES No access permission. 
-22 -EINVAL Invalid argument was specified.  
-28 -ENOSPC No free space.

low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following values.

 PlayStation File System (for EE) - ioctl2 Commands    5-61 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
PIOCATTRREAD                                             
Read extended file attribute entry  
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
pfs 2.2.2 December 1, 2003 
Arguments 
arg  Reserved. Specify NULL. 
arglen Size of arg. 
bufp  512 byte buffer that will store the key and value 
buflen  Size of bufp. 
Description 
Copies the next entry from the attribute entry stream into bufp. Returns 0 when reaches the end of entries.  
Example: 
        struct { 
          char key[0x100]; 
          char value[0x100]; 
        } attr; 
        while ((r = sceIoctl2(fd, PIOCATTRREAD, NULL, 0, &attr, 0)) > 0) 
          printf("%s/% s\n", attr.key, attr.value); 
Return value 
If processing is successful, returns lthe ength of the key. Returns zero if reaches the end of entries. 
If there is an error, returns one of the following: 
Table 5-57 
Value Macro Result 
-5 -EIO I/O error. 
-12 -ENOMEM Not enough free memory. 
 
Error values can be converted to sceerrno.h error codes by using the sceStdioConvertError() function. The 
low-order 16-bits of the error code can have the following values. 
Table 5-58 
Before Conversion Macro after Conversion Converted Value 
-EIO SCE_EIO 0x0005 
-ENOMEM SCE_ENOMEM 0x000c 
See also 
sceStdioConvertError()

Structure for generating icon.sys 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified

Structure 
typedef struct {  
     unsigned char Head[4]; 'P','S','2','D' 
     unsigned short Reserv1; Reserved area, must be filled entirely with 00 
     unsigned short OffsLF; Line break position in title name 
     unsigned int Reserv2; Reserved area, must be filled entirely with 00  
     unsigned int TransRate; Background transparency 
     _iconVu0IVECTOR BgColor[4]; Background color (4 points) 
     _iconVu0FVECTOR LightDir[3]; Light source direction (3 light sources) 
     _iconVu0FVECTOR LightColor[3]; Light source color (3 light sources) 
     _iconVu0FVECTOR Ambient; Ambient light 
     unsigned char TitleName[68]; Title name 
     unsigned char FnameView[64]; List icon file name 
     unsigned char FnameCopy[64]; Copy icon file name 
     unsigned char FnameDel[64]; Deletion icon file name 
     unsigned char Reserve3[512]; Reserved area, must be filled entirely with 00  
} sceMcIconSys;  
 
Note: _iconVu0IVECTOR structure is the same as that of sceVu0IVECTOR in libvu0.h, without an 
alignment declaration. _iconVu0FVECTOR structure is the same as that of sceVu0FVECTOR in libvu0.h, 
without an alignment declaration. 
Description 
This structure generates icon.sys.

6-16    Memory Card Library - File/Directory Control Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
libmc.h:   
Subdirectory sceMcFileAttrSubdir 0x0020 
File write completed sceMcFileAttrClosed 0x0080 
PDA application 
(1st generation PDA 
download) 
sceMcFileAttrPDAExec 0x0800 
PlayStation format data sceMcFileAttrPS1 0x1000 
(Definitions of SCE_STM_*** are in sifdev.h) 
Method of using wildcard characters: 
• If "player01.score.0304" is sent as name, only the entry "player01.score.0304" matches. 
• If "?" is entered for part of name, a match occurs for any single character corresponding to the ? 
portion. For example, if "player??.score.????" is sent, "player01.score.0304", 
"player02.score.1203", and "player03.score." are all matches. If ? is in the middle of a character 
string and no character appears there, the entry will not match. However, if ? is at the end of a 
character string, then an entry that ends just before it will also match. 
• If "*" is entered for part of name, a match occurs for any character string of any length 
corresponding to the "*" portion.For example, if name is "*", all entries match, including even 
entries that begin with ".". 
• "*" can be entered more than once in a charact er string. For example, if "*score*" is sent, 
"player01.score.0304", "1-score1203", and "score" all match. 
• "*" and "?" can also be combined. 
Notes 
sceMcFileAttrClosed is an attribute that can be used to confirm the completion of a write operation. 
This attribute indicates that operations on a file have completed and that data written has been reflected 
onto the Memory Card.  This would occur after sceMcWrite() has been executed, and before sceMcClose() 
or sceMcFlush(), provided that the Memory Card has not been removed. 
There is no change to the attribute if sceMcWrite() isn't executed between an sceMcOpen() and 
sceMcClose(). 
Conversely, if a file does not have this attribute set, data may not be completely reflected onto the card if 
the card is removed or an error occurs while the file is being written.  
This attribute can be used with Release 1.4.6 or later.  Since a file that was written using a library earlier 
than Release 1.4.6 will not have this attribute, you cannot check this attribute to confirm that a write 
operation has completed. 
For the current Release 2.2, in the browser of the domestic version of the actual PlayStation 2 and that of 
the DTL-H10000, the sceMcFileAttrClosed attribute (Closed flag) does not get copied while copying a file. 
This will become part of the browser specification. 
The following events can be inferred from the Closed flag: 
Flag 1: Writing completed normally. 
Flag 0: 'possible' error during writing. 
Therefore, during data check processing if the application determines that 'an error occurred because the 
Closed flag is 0', then processing should not be considered correct. 
The Closed flag is only provided for purposes of support.  
(We recommend that to decide whether the final contents of the file are correct, a separate mechanism 
such as a checksum should be implemented on the application side.)

Calling conditions

Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description

The subdirectory can be specified by using either an absolute pathname or a relative pathname from the 
current directory. If an absolute pathname is specified, the subdirectories above the subdirectory to be 
created must already exist.

6-20    Memory Card Library - File/Directory Control Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcOpen 
Open file 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc 1.1 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceMcOpen(  
 int port, Port number (0/1) 
 int slot, Slot number 
 const char *name, File name 
 int mode) Readable: 1, Writable: 2, Newly created: 0x200 
(The value passed is the logical OR of the required mode properties) 
These are defined as follows in sifdev.h: 
  SCE_RDONLY    0x0001 
  SCE_WRONLY   0x0002 
  SCE_RDWR       0x0003 
  SCE_CREAT      0x0200 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
Opens a file and returns the file descriptor. Up to three files can be open at the same time for all ports. 
name can include a path, but without the path, name must be less than 31 characters. 
Table 6-20: Value returned in result of sceMcSync()

If the value of result returned by sceMcSync() is negative, the file on which the operation was attempted is

6-24    Memory Card Library - File/Directory Control Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcReadFast 
Read file fast 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc 3.0.3 June 9, 2005 
Syntax 
int sceMcReadFast(  
 int fd, File handler number 
 void *buff, Pointer to buffer for writing data that was read 
 int size) Size to be read (unit: bytes) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This is the fast version of sceMcRead(). Its basic specifications are the same as those of sceMcRead(). 
sceMcReadFast() reads data approximately 2 to 3 times faster than sceMcRead(), however, this depends 
somewhat on how fragmented the file is. In addition, since the data is read directly from the Memory Card 
without passing through the file cache, the speed should increase as the value of size increases.

-5 sceMcResDeniedPermit File handler could not be opened in 
read mode  
Or, because a 128 KB Memory Card 
was inserted, it could not be accessed 
-10 or less  
(-11 to -19,  
-40 to -49,  
-50 to -59,  
-70 to -79) 
 Memory Card could not be detected

Update file information 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc 1.1 July 19, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceMcSetFileInfo(  
 int port, Port number 
 int slot, Slot number 
 const char *name, File name 
 const char *info File information to be written 
 unsigned int valid) Set 1 for bits corresponding to information you want to set  
Creation date/time: sceMcFileInfoCreate       0x01 
Modification date/time: sceMcFileInfoModify  0x02 
File attribute: sceMcFileInfoAttr                      0x04 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
Overwrites the system information of a file. 
Only the attributes for a directory can be changed. File attributes cannot be changed. 
Data contents are stored in table as follows (the same format as the first half of sceMcTblGetDir). 
Table 6-35 
   +7           +6       +5 +4 +3 +2 +1 +0  
+00h Year Month Day Hour Minute Second -- Creation date/time 
+08h Year Month Day Hour Minute Second -- Modification date/time 
+10h ---- Attribute ---- 
 
Table 6-36: Valid bits in file attribute: 
libmc.h:   
Readable sceMcFileAttrReadable 0x0001 
Writable sceMcFileAttrWriteable 0x0002 
Executable sceMcFileAttrExecutable 0x0004 
Copy prohibited sceMcFileAttrDupProhibit 0x0008 
PDA application 
(1st generation PDA 
download) 
sceMcFileAttrPDAExec 0x0800 
PlayStation format data sceMcFileAttrPS1 0x1000 
(Definitions of SCE_STM_*** are in sifdev.h)

6-32    Memory Card Library - File/Directory Control Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcWrite 
Write to file 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc 1.1 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceMcWrite(  
 int fd, File handler number 
 const void *buff, Pointer to buffer where data to be written is stored 
 int size) Size to be written (unit:bytes) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
Writes data to a file on the Memory Card. As long as the write data does not exceed a size that can be 
maintained in the file cache, it only remains in the file cache and is not reflected on the Memory Card. If you 
want to immediately reflect it on the Memory Card, call sceMcFlush(). (The file cache size is 24K bytes.) 
Table 6-40: Value returned in result of sceMcSync() 
Value Macro Result 
0 or more  Number of bytes that were actually 
written (success)  
-2 sceMcResNoFormat Memory card was unformatted  
-3 sceMcResFullDevice Data could not be written due to 
insufficient free space  
-4 sceMcResNoEntry File handler has not been opened  
-5 sceMcResDeniedPermit File handler could not be opened in 
write mode  
Or, because a 128 KB Memory Card 
was inserted, it could not be accessed 
-8 sceMcResFailReplace Attempte d to use the subrogation area 
when writing failed, but writing to the 
subrogation area failed repeatedly and 
ultimately data could not be written 
-10 or less  
(-11 to -19,  
-40 to -49,  
-50 to -59,  
-70 to -79) 
 Memory Card could not be detected 
 
The error value can be converted to an sceerrno.h error code by using sceMcConvertError 
(SCE_MC_FUNC_WRITE, ...). The low-order 16 bits of the converted error code indicate one of the 
following values.

6-34    Memory Card Library - File/Directory Control Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcWriteFast 
Write to file fast 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc 3.0.3 June 9, 2005 
Syntax 
int sceMcWriteFast(  
 int fd, File handler number 
 const void *buff, Pointer to buffer where data to be written is stored 
 int size) Size to be written (unit:bytes) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This is the fast version of sceMcWrite(). Its basic specifications are the same as those of sceMcWrite(). 
sceMcWriteFast() writes data to a file on the Memory Card approximately twice as fast as sceMcWrite(). 
Since data is written directly to the Memory Card without passing through the file cache, as the value of 
size decreases, performance will also decrease. As a general rule, if size is greater than 64KB, 
sceMcWriteFast() will normally write data faster than sceMcWrite(). 
Table 6-43: Value returned in result of sceMcSync() 
Value Macro Result

-5 sceMcResDeniedPermit File handler could not be opened in 
write mode  
Or, because a 128 KB Memory Card 
was inserted, it could not be accessed 
-8 sceMcResFailReplace Attempte d to use the subrogation area 
when writing failed, but writing to the 
subrogation area failed repeatedly and 
ultimately data could not be written 
-10 or less  
(-11 to -19,

sceMcResNoEntry SCE_EBADF 0x0009 
sceMcResDeniedPermit SCE_EACCES 0x000d 
sceMcResFailReplace SCE_EIO 0x0005 
-10 or less SCE_ENODEV 0x0013 
(-11 to -19,   
-40 to -49,   
-50 to -59,   
-70 to -79)   
Notes 
If the result of sceMcSync() is a negative number, the file that was about to be manipulated is forcibly 
closed. 
If writing fails, there is no guarantee that the data actually got written to the Memory Card. 
Since this function makes use of the SIO2 resource, other devices that use the SIO2 such as the controller 
or the Multitap will enter a communication stopped state when this function is executed.

-200 sceMcErrSemapho The process could not be registered because 
another process was executing.

===== PAGE 265 =====

 Memory Card Library - Memory Card Control Functions 6-41             
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Before Conversion Macro after  Conversion Converted Value 
-50 to -59,   
-70 to -79)   
 
When entries are to be created in a given directory, this function checks the number of entries that can be 
created without taking up new Memory Card capacity.  Use this function when you want to calculate the 
Memory Card capacity to be taken up by creating entries.  
Entry information takes up one cluster (1 KB) of Memory Card storage per two entries.   
If an existing directory contains an odd number of entries, the 512 bytes in the last half of the last cluster 
will be empty, and one entry information storage area will be free.  
If the number of entries is even and no entry has been deleted, exactly the required amount of space for 
entry information storage areas will have been allocated.  Therefore, the number of free entry information 
storage areas will be zero, and one cluster will be required to generate a new entry. 
Once an entry information storage area is allocated, its size will not be reduced until the directory in which 
that entry information is being managed is deleted.  Therefore, if five entries are eliminated in a directory 
that now has zero empty entry information storage areas, the number of empty storage areas will increase 
to five at that time.  
For example, if c new entries are created in a directory that has f empty entry information storage areas, 
Memory Card storage that will be taken up by the entry information will be ((c-f)/2) rounded up to the 
nearest integer. 
Return value 
Table 6-54 
Value Macro Result 
0  Processing succeeded (the process was 
registered) 
-91 sceMcErrSifRpc The process coul d not be registered due to an 
error  
-100 sceMcErrUnbind sceMcInit() was not executed. 
-200 sceMcErrSemapho The process could not be registered because 
another process was executing. 
-210 sceMcErrNullStr The process co uld not be registered because a 
NULL pointer or a zero-byte length string was 
passed to the pathname 
See also 
sceMcConvertError()

Library Introduced Documentation last modified

 int level) Thread priority 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
Changes the thread priority of mcserv.irx, the IOP module of the PDA library. Possible priority settings are 
values in the range of USER_HIGHEST_PRIORITY - USER_LOWEST_PRIORITY (inclusive), as defined in 
thread.h. The initial thread priority value for mcserv.irx is 104. 
Table 6-62: Return value in result of sceMcSync()  (This macro is defined in kerror.h) 
Value Macro Result 
0 KE_OK Success 
-403 KE_ILLEGAL_PRIORITY Thread priority value exceeds valid range 
 
The error value can be converted to an sceerrno.h error code by using sceMcConvertError 
(SCE_MC_FUNC_CHANGETHREADPRIORITY, ...). The low-order 16 bits of the converted error code 
indicate one of the following values. 
Table 6-63 
Before Conversion Macro after  Conversion Converted Value 
sceMcErrSifRpc SCE_ERPC 0x800a 
sceMcErrUnbind SCE_EINIT 0x8001 
sceMcErrSemapho SCE_EBUSY 0x0010 
KE_ILLEGAL_PRIORITY SCE_EINVAL 0x0016 
Notes 
The thread priority can also be set using sceSifLoadModule() when mcserv.irx is loaded. For example, if 
mcserv.irx is loaded as shown below, the initial thread priority will be 100. The thread priority string should 
be expressed as a decimal value. 
unsigned char *param = "thpri=100"; 
sceSifLoadModule( "host0:/usr/local/sce/iop/modules/mcserv.irx", strlen(param)+1, param);

6-48    Memory Card Library - Library System Functions  
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcGetErxEntries 
Get library entries 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
void *sceMcGetErxEntries (void);  
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets a pointer to the SceErxLibraryHeader structure which is needed to register the libmc 
library in liberx. 
If the SceErxLibraryHeader structure returned by this function is passed to the 
sceErxRegisterLibraryEntries() function of liberx, the statically linked libmc library can be called from an ERX 
module (can be dynamically linked).  
When this function is linked, the entire implementation of libmc will be linked via the library entry table of 
liberx. 
In some cases, this may also increase the size of the program. 
Return value 
The entry library for libmc is returned. 
See also 
sceErxInit() 
 

===== PAGE 273 =====

 Memory Card Library - Library System Functions 6-49             
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcGetSlotMax                       
Get number of slots 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc 2.0 July 19, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceMcGetSlotMax(  
 int port) Port number 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
sceMcGetSlotMax() itself is a synchronous function. However, it uses the same IOP call channel as other 
libmc functions so it cannot get the slot count while other asynchronous functions are running. 
Note that there may be a discrepancy between the information obtained from this function and information 
obtained from the sceMtapGetConnection() function because of timing related to the multitap connection. 
The multitap connection status and number of slots can also be obtained at the same time by using the 
sceMtapGetInfo() function, which is implemented in the multitap library. 
For functions that require a slot number, a value from 0 to (slot count - 1) can be provided. Slots A - D of 
the SCPH-10090 multitap correspond to slot numbers 0 - 3, respectively. 
Return value 
Table 6-65 
Value Macro Result 
1-4  The number of the usable slots in the port  
-91 sceMcErrSifRpc The number of slots could not be determined 
due to an error  
-100 sceMcErrUnbind sceMcInit() was not executed. 
-200 sceMcErrSemapho The number of slots could not be determined 
because another process was executing. 
 
The error value can be converted to an sceerrno.h error code by using sceMcConvertError

 Memory Card Library - Error Conversion Function 6-53             
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Description 
With the Memory Card library, the same error number may produce different conversion results, depending 
on the function number. In particular, note that the converted error will be different for sceMcResNoEntry, 
sceMcResDeniedPermit, sceMcResFullDevice, and sceMcResNotEmpty. 
Return value 
The error value returned from a Memory Card library function is converted to an sceerrno.h error code by 
this function and returned. 
The prefix used to identify the Memory Card library is SCE_ERROR_PREFIX_MC.

Structures 
SceMc2DateParam 
Date structure 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
      unsigned char reserved; Reserved (0x00) 
      unsigned char sec; Seconds 
      unsigned char min; Minutes 
      unsigned char hour; Hours 
      unsigned char day; Day 
      unsigned char month; Month 
      unsigned short year; Year 
} SceMc2DateParam;  
Description 
This structure is used for storing date information. 
See also 
SceMcDir2Param 
 

===== PAGE 284 =====

7-4  Memory Card Library 2 - Structures 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
SceMc2DirParam 
Directory / file information structure 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
      SceMc2DateParam creation; Creation date 
      SceMc2DateParam modification; Modification date 
      unsigned int size; File size 
      unsigned short attribute; Attribute 
      unsigned short resv1;  
      char name[32]; Filename 
} SceMc2DirParam;  
Description 
This structure is used for storing directory and file information. 
See also 
sceMc2GetDir(), sceMc2GetDirAsync(), sceMc2SearchFile(), sceMc2SearchFileAsync(), 
 

===== PAGE 285 =====

 Memory Card Library 2 - Structures    7-5
  
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
SceMc2InfoParam 
Memory Card information structure 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
      int type; Media type 
1: 128KB Memory Card or PDA 
2: Memory Card (PS2) 
      int isFormat; 1: Formatted 
0: Unformatted 
      int freeClust; Number of free clusters 
      }  SceMc2InfoParam;  
Description 
This structure is used for storing the state information of a Memory Card. 
With the current version, type cannot be set to 1 (128KB Memory Card or PDA).  
If a 128KB Memory Card or PDA is inserted, the library will infer that no Memory Card is inserted. 
See also 
sceMc2GetInfo(), sceMc2GetInfoAsync()  
 

===== PAGE 286 =====

7-6  Memory Card Library 2 - Structures 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
SceMc2SocketParam 
Virtual socket parameter structure 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 December 6, 2002 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
      unsigned int option; Socket option 
      int port; Port number (2 or 3) 
      int slot; Slot number (always 0) 
      int number; Device number (unused) 
      char name[SCE_MC2_MAX_DEVICE_NAME]; Device name (unused) 
} SceMc2SocketParam;  
Description 
This structure is used for storing virtual socket parameters. 
Since the current version does not support multitaps, slot must always be 0.  
If a value other than 0 is entered, an error will occur. 
See also 
sceMc2CreateSocket()

sceMc2Init 
Initialize library 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 January 22, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceMc2Init (  
 int mode) Always 0 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function initializes the library. 
Return value 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-1 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Success 
SCE_ESEMA Semaphore creation failed 
SCE_ETHREAD Thread creation failed

 Memory Card Library 2 - DBC Socket Functions    7-11
  
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMc2SearchSocket 
Find socket number 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
Libmc2 2.8 November 20, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceMc2SearchSocket(  
int port, Port number 
(Specify SCE_MC2_PORT_1 or SCE_MC2_PORT_2) 
int slot) Socket number (Fixed at 0) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
This function finds the Memory Card inserted in the specified port and slot, and returns its MC2 socket 
number. 
Internally this function performs a synchronous SIFRPC, so the time needed to execute the function may 
vary depending on the load on the IOP. 
If you call this function while executing an asynchronous function, SCE_EBUSY will be returned. You should 
check to see if any other functions are executing by first calling sceMc2CheckAsync(). 
Return value 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-5 
Value Result 
>= 0 Success (value is MC2 socket number) 
SCE_EBUSY Could not register the func tion because another function is 
executing 
SCE_EID Could not find corresponding socket 
SCE_EINIT sceMc2Init() could not be executed 
See also 
sceMc2CheckAsync() 
 

===== PAGE 292 =====

7-12  Memory Card Library 2 – File/Directory Control Functions     
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
File/Directory Control Functions 
sceMc2Chdir 
Change current directory / get current directory 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 January 22, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceMc2Chdir (  
 int socket_num, Socket number 
 const char *path, Subdirectory path of destination 
 char *pwd) Pointer to buffer for storing current directory. 
Specify NULL if not used. 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function changes the current directory. 
The destination directory may be specified by using an absolute path or a relative path from the current 
directory. 
Specifying "." in the directory name means the current directory and specifying ".." means the parent 
directory of the current directory. 
If this function is called while an asynchronous function is executing, SCE_EBUSY will be returned. Use 
sceMc2CheckAsync() to check the function that is executing. 
Return value 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-6 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Process was registered 
SCE_EBUSY Process could not be registered because 
another function is executing 
SCE_EID Socket number is invalid 
SCE_ENAMETOOLONG Path name is too long 
SCE_EINIT sceMc2Init() has not been executed 
SCE_ENOENT Specified path does not exist 
SCE_ENOTDIR Specified pa th is not a directory 
SCE_EINVAL Path name is invalid 
SCE_ECONNREFUSED Memory Card access failed 

===== PAGE 293 =====

 Memory Card Library 2 - File/Directory Control Functions    7-13
  
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Value Result 
SCE_ENODEV Memory Card could not be detected 
 
See also 
sceMc2ChdirAsync(), sceMc2CheckAsync()

Library Introduced Documentation last modified

int sceMc2ChdirAsync (

 char *pwd) Pointer to buffer for storing current directory. 
Specify NULL if not used. 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state)

directory. 
Specifying "." in the directory name means the current directory and specifying ".." means the parent 
directory of the current directory. 
Since this function is asynchronous, it completes immediately. To check whether the function has 
completed, use the sceMc2CheckAsync() or sceMc2Sync() functions. 
Value returned in rslt of sceMc2Sync() or sceMc2CheckAsync(): 
Table 7-7 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Success 
SCE_ENOENT Specified path does not exist 
SCE_ENOTDIR Specified pa th is not a directory 
SCE_EINVAL Path name is invalid 
SCE_ECONNREFUSED Memory Card access failed 
SCE_ENODEV Memory Card could not be detected 
Return value

depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-8 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Process was registered 
SCE_EBUSY Process could not be registered because 
another function is executing 
SCE_EID Socket number is invalid 
SCE_ENAMETOOLONG Path name is too long

sceMc2Chmod 
Change directory attribute 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 March 31, 2004 
Syntax 
int sceMc2Chmod (  
 int socket_num, Socket number 
 const char* name, Directory name 
 unsigned short attr) Directory attribute to be set 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function changes the attributes of a directory. 
Only directory attributes can be changed. File attributes cannot be changed. 
Valid bits in the file attribute specification are defined as follows in libmc2.h. 
Table 7-9 
Macro Value Attribute 
SCE_MC2_FILE_ATTR_READABLE 0x0001  Readable 
SCE_MC2_FILE_ATTR_WRITEABLE 0x0002  Writeable 
SCE_MC2_FILE_ATTR_EXECUTABLE 0x0004 Executable 
SCE_MC2_FILE_ATTR_DUPPROHIBIT 0x0008  Copy prohibited 
 
If this function is called while an asynchronous function is executing, SCE_EBUSY will be returned. Use 
sceMc2CheckAsync() to check the function that is executing. 
Return value 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-10 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Success 
SCE_EBUSY Process could not be registered because 
another function is executing 
SCE_EID Socket number is invalid 
SCE_ENAMETOOLONG Path name is too long 
SCE_ECONNREFUSED Memory Card access failed 
SCE_EINVAL Path name is invalid 
SCE_ENOENT Specified path does not exist 
SCE_ENODEV Memory Card could not be detected

sceMc2GetDir 
Get Memory Card file list 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 January 22, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceMc2GetDir (  
 int socket_num,  Socket number 
 const char* name, Path name of files for which file list is to be obtained 
 int ofs, Entry offset. 
Specifies the number of leading entries to skip (entries that 
will not be stored in *dir) among the detected file group. 
 int maxent, Maximum number of file entries that may be written in *dir 
by one call. 
If a negative number is specified, only the number of 
detected entries is stored in count and nothing will be 
written to *dir. 
 SceMc2DirParam* dir, Pointer to buffer for storing the detected file information 
 int* count) Pointer to buffer for storing the number of detected entries 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets a list of files present on the Memory Card. 
If this function is called while an asynchronous function is executing, SCE_EBUSY will be returned. Use 
sceMc2CheckAsync() to check the function that is executing. 
Return value 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-20 
Value Result 
SCE_OK Success 
SCE_EBUSY Process could not be registered because 
another function is executing 
SCE_EID Socket number is invalid 
SCE_ENAMETOOLONG Path name is too long 
SCE_ECONNREFUSED Memory Card access failed 
SCE_EINVAL Path name is invalid 
SCE_EFILE_BROKEN Specified  directory is corrupted 
SCE_ENOENT Specified path does not exist 
SCE_ENOTDIR Specified pa th is not a directory 
SCE_ENODEV Memory Card could not be detected 
SCE_EINIT SceMc2Init() has not been executed

7-30  Memory Card Library 2 – File/Directory Control Functions     
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMc2ReadFastFile 
Read file fast 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 3.0.3 June 9, 2005 
Syntax 
int sceMc2ReadFastFile (  
 int socket_num, Socket number 
 const char* name, Filename 
 void* bufs, Pointer to buffer for storing read data 
 int ofs, Offset from beginning where reading is to begin (bytes) 
 unsigned int size) Amount of data to read (bytes) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This is the fast version of sceMc2ReadFile(). 
sceMc2ReadFastFile() reads data from a file on the Memory Card approximately 2 to 3 times faster than 
sceMc2ReadFile(). 
The speed should increase as the value of size increases. 
Note that the data that was read may only get written to the cache, so a function such as SyncDCache() 
should be called first to flush the cache when it is necessary to directly perform a DMA transfer of the data 
that was read. 
If this function is called while an asynchronous function is executing, SCE_EBUSY will be returned. Use 
sceMc2CheckAsync() to check the function that is executing. 
Notes 
Since this function makes use of the SIO2 resource, other devices that use the SIO2 such as the controller 
or the Multitap will enter a communication stopped state when this function is executed. 
Return value 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-26 
Value Result 
0 or more Success (value is number of bytes read) 
SCE_EBUSY Process could not be registered because 
another function is executing 
SCE_EID Socket number is invalid 
SCE_ENAMETOOLONG Path name is too long 
SCE_ECONNREFUSED Memory Card access failed 
SCE_EINVAL Path name or parameter is invalid 

===== PAGE 311 =====

 Memory Card Library 2 - File/Directory Control Functions    7-31
  
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Value Result 
SCE_EACCES File is read-prohibited 
SCE_ENOENT Specified path does not exist 
SCE_ENODEV Memory Card could not be detected 
SCE_EINIT sceMc2Init() has not been executed 
See also 
sceMc2ReadFileAsync(), sceMc2CheckAsync()

sceMc2WriteFastFile 
Write to file fast 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 3.0.3 June 9, 2005 
Syntax 
int sceMc2WriteFastFile (  
 int socket_num, Socket number 
 const char* name, Filename 
 const void* bufs, Pointer to buffer which contains data to be written 
 int ofs, Offset from beginning where writing is to begin (bytes) 
 unsigned int size) Amount of data to be written (bytes) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This is the fast version of sceMc2WriteFile(). 
sceMc2WriteFastFile() writes data to a file on the Memory Card approximately four times faster than 
sceMc2WriteFile(). 
If this function is called while an asynchronous function is executing, SCE_EBUSY will be returned. Use 
sceMc2CheckAsync() to check the function that is executing. 
Notes 
Since this function makes use of the SIO2 resource, other devices that use the SIO2 such as the controller 
or the Multitap will enter a communication stopped state when this function is executed. 
Since data is written directly to the Memory Card without passing through the file cache, as the value of 
size decreases, performance will also decrease. 
Return value 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-38 
Value Result 
0 or more Success (value is number of bytes written) 
SCE_EBUSY Process could not be registered because 
another function is executing 
SCE_EID Socket number is invalid 
SCE_ENAMETOOLONG Path name is too long 
SCE_ECONNREFUSED Memory Card access failed 
SCE_EINVAL Path name or parameter is invalid 
SCE_EACCES File is write-prohibited. 
SCE_ENOSPC Insufficient free space

7-50  Memory Card Library 2 – Memory Card Control Functions     
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMc2GetEntSpace 
Check for free space in entry information storage area 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmc2 2.7 January 22, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceMc2GetEntSpace (  
 int socket_num, Socket number 
 const char* name) Path name to be checked 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in interrupt enabled state) 
Description 
This function finds the number of entries that can be created in a directory without using any new clusters. 
It is typically used to calculate how much Memory Card space will be used when creating new directory 
entries. 
One cluster (1 KB) of Memory Card space is required to store information for every two entries. 
When an existing directory contains an odd number of entries, the last 512 bytes of the final cluster will be 
empty, so there will be one vacancy in the entry information storage area. 
When there are an even number of entries but no deleted entries, exactly the required amount of space will 
be allocated for the entry information storage area. In this case, the number of vacancies is zero and one 
cluster will be needed to create a new entry. 
Once the entry information storage area is allocated, its size will not decrease until the directory for which 
the entry information is being managed is deleted. In other words, if five entries are deleted in a directory 
that currently has no vacancies, the number of vacancies will increase to five at that time. 
For example, if an attempt is made to create c new entries in a directory that has f vacancies in the entry 
information storage area, the amount of new Memory Card space that will be used for the entry information 
is ((c - f)/2) rounded up. 
If this function is called while an asynchronous function is executing, SCE_EBUSY will be returned. Use 
sceMc2CheckAsync() to check the function that is executing. 
Return value 
When an error occurs, the low-order 16 bits of the return value indicate one of the following values 
depending on the type of error. 
Table 7-47 
Value Result 
0 or more Success (number of free entries is returned)  
SCE_EBUSY Process Process could not be registered 
because another function is executing 
SCE_EID Socket number is invalid 
SCE_ENAMETOOLONG Path name is too long 
SCE_ECONNREFUSED Memory Card access failed 

===== PAGE 331 =====

Value Result 
SCE_EINVAL Path name or parameter is invalid 
SCE_ENOENT Specified path does not exist 
SCE_ENOTDIR Specified pa th is not a directory 
SCE_ENODEV Memory Card could not be detected 
SCE_EINIT sceMc2Init() has not been executed

 Memory Card Library 2 - Library System Functions    7-59
  
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMc2GetErxEntries 
Get library entries 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
Libmc2 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
void *sceMc2GetErxEntries (void);  
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets a pointer to the SceErxLibraryHeader structure which is needed to register the libmc2 
library in liberx. 
If the SceErxLibraryHeader structure returned by this function is passed to the 
sceErxRegisterLibraryEntries() function of liberx, the statically linked libmc2 library can be called from an 
ERX module (can be dynamically linked).  
When this function is linked, the entire implementation of libmc2 will be linked via the library entry table of 
liberx. 
In some cases, this may also increase the size of the program. 
Return value 
The entry library for libmc2 is returned. 
See also 
sceErxInit()

sceMcxTblInfo       
Structure for passing PDA information   
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmcx 2.0 October 6, 2000 
Structure 
typedef struct {  
      short AppliNo; PDA application number to execute or being executed 
      short Reserve1; Reserved area. Must be filled with 00. 
      int AplArg; Arguments to pass to PDA application 
      unsigned char Speaker; Disable speaker 
      unsigned char Infrared; Disable infrared communications/remote control 
transmission 
      unsigned char Flash; Inhibit PDA application from writing to Flash memory 
      unsigned char Led; Disable LED 
      struct {  
                 unsigned char Week,; RTC (real time clock) day of the week 
                 unsigned char Sec RTC seconds 
                 unsigned char Min RTC minutes 
                 unsigned char Hour RTC hours 
                 unsigned char Day RTC day 
                 unsigned char Month; RTC month 
                 unsigned short Year; RTC year 
      } _Rtc;  
      unsigned Serial;  
}  sceMcxTblInfo;  
 
Table 8-1 
#define Member 
#define PWeek _Rtc.Week 
#define PSec _Rtc.Sec 
#define PMin _Rtc.Min 
#define PHour _Rtc.Hour 
#define PDay _Rtc.Day 
#define PMonth _Rtc.Month 
#define PYear _Rtc.Year

8-8    PDA Library - PDA Control Functions     
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
PDA Control Functions 
sceMcxGetInfo                         
Get PDA information   
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmcx 2.0 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceMcxGetInfo(  
 int port, Port number 
 int slot, Slot number 
 sceMcxTblInfo *info) Pointer to buffer for storing PDA information 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function allows the following values stored in the PDA to be accessed: 
• Application number of PDA application being executed 
The starting block of the application being executed on the PDA is returned. The block referred to here 
is in file management units (8 KB) in the flash memory area of the PlayStation memory card. If the 
launcher application is executing, 0 is returned. If another application is executing, a value of 1 - 15 is 
returned. 
• Disabled state of [Speaker output/infrared transmission/PDA application flash write/LED] 
If a feature is disabled, a 1 is returned. If enabled, a 0 is returned. 
• Real-time clock 
The real-time clock in the PDA is read and the year/month/day/minutes/seconds/day of the week is 
returned. 
• Serial number 
The upper 8 bits represent a single ASCII character, and the lower 24 bits represent an 8-digit decimal 
number. The two are combined to form a serial number such as A00000001. 
 
Return value in result of sceMcxSync(): 
Table 8-3 
Value Macro Result 
0 sceMcxResSucceed Successful 
-12 sceMcxResNoDevice PDA was not detected 

===== PAGE 351 =====

 PDA Library - PDA Control Functions    8-9 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Return value 
Table 8-4 
Value Result 
0 Operation was registered 
1 or greater The function number  (s ceMcxFuncNo.....) of the function being 
executed is returned. The operation could not be registered 
because another process was running. 
-1 or less The operation could not be registered due to an error.

8-10    PDA Library - PDA Control Functions     
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcxGetMem                           
Read PDA memory      
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmcx 2.0 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceMcxGetMem(  
 int port, Port number 
 int slot, Slot number 
 void *buff, Pointer to buffer for reading memory contents 
 unsigned addr, Starting address of memory to be read 
 unsigned size) Number of bytes to read (128 bytes maximum) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
The specified number of bytes of PDA memory beginning at the specified address is read and stored in the 
buffer. A PDA bus error will be generated if the address is not in the ranges shown below or if an attempt is 
made to access 0x2****** addresses and virtual flash memory has not been set up. 
Readable ranges: 
0x0******, 0x2******, 0x4******, 0x6******, 0x8****** 
0xA******, 0xB******, 0xC******, 0xD****** (****** can be any 6 hexadecimal digits) 
 
Return value in result of sceMcxSync(): 
Table 8-5 
Value Macro Result 
0 sceMcxResSucceed Successful 
-12 sceMcxResNoDevice PDA was not detected 
Return value 
Table 8-6 
Value Result 
0 Operation was registered 
1 or greater The function number  (s ceMcxFuncNo.....) of the function being 
executed is returned. The operation could not be registered 
because another process was running. 
-1 or less The operation could not be registered due to an error.

8-16    PDA Library - PDA Control Functions     
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcxSetInfo                    
Update PDA information     
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmcx 2.0 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceMcxSetInfo(  
 int port, Port number 
 int slot, Slot number 
 const sceMcxTblInfo *info, Pointer to buffer for storing update contents 
 unsigned part) Parameters to be updated 
6 types of parameters can be updated.  
The following are the bits corresponding to the 
parameters to be updated (definitions in libmcx.h) 
sceMcxBitAppli  PDA application execution 
sceMcxBitSpeaker Speaker disabled state 
sceMcxBitInfrared Infrared communications/remote
                                       control disabled state 
sceMcxBitFlash  PDA application flash write 
                                       disabled state 
sceMcxBitLed  LED disabled state 
sceMcxBitDate  Current time of real-time clock  
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function updates PDA information. 
• PDA application execution 
A PDA application to be executed is specified for the AppliNo member using the starting block number 
stored in the PDA. The values set up in AplArg are passed to the application as arguments. The block 
referred to here is in file management units (8 KB) in the flash memory area of the PlayStation memory 
card. To run the launcher application, specify 0. To run another application, specify a value of 1 - 15. 
To determine the starting block number, first use sceMcxGetDir() to check the PDA application file 
information. As a result, the value stored in table ->PdaAplNo is the application number. 
When a PDA application is to be run, the PlayStation sends an end application request to the currently 
running PDA application. This request is sent as "bit 11: end PDA application" in the result for "Get 
PDA status (swi 6)" (this flag must be monitored periodically). 
For information about what operations a PDA application needs to perform when it exits, refer to the 
"PDA Kernel Specification". 
When the parameter is updated, an end request will be sent to the currently running PDA application. A 
running application may refuse to exit though, so sceMcxGetInfo() should be used to confirm that the 
PDA application has switched. (However, when there is no communication between the PDA 
application and the PlayStation 2, the application number of the currently running application cannot be 
determined). 

===== PAGE 359 =====

 PDA Library - PDA Control Functions    8-17 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
• [Speaker output/infrared transmission/LED] disabled state 1 to disable a feature, 0 to enable. 
These controls are provided due to the limited current capacity that is available to the front-panel 
terminals on the PlayStation 2. All features are disabled by default when the PDA is first plugged into 
the PlayStation 2. 
The table below shows the current consumption of the different modules. 
The maximum current that can be supplied by the PlayStation 2 is 160 mA total for two ports, so 
adjustments should be made to prevent exceeding this value(particularly when using multitaps). 
Table 8-15 
Module name Current consumption  
CPU chip  10mA                 
IR module transmission 70mA                 
Speaker 20mA                 
LED 10mA                 
 
"Get PDA status (swi 6)"  should be used to check the usage restriction state of these three features 
when using them in a PDA application. 
(For information about what operations PDA applications need to perform, please refer to the "PDA 
Kernel Specification"). 
 
• PDA application flash write disabled state 
1 to disable PDA flash memory writes by a PDA application. 0 to enable.  
If a PDA application is writing to flash memory and communication with the PlayStation 2 takes place, 
problems may develop in the PDA's internal processing and there may be access conflicts. This setting 
is provided so that writing to flash may be disabled because communication between a PlayStation 2 
program and the PDA may fail. 
However, this enable/disable setting is reported to the PDA application only as the result of "Get PDA 
status (swi 6)". Thus, if a PDA application is to write to flash memory, it must use "Get PDA status (swi 
6)" to see that writing is possible. (For information about what operations PDA applications are required 
to perform, please refer to the "PDA Kernel Specification".) 
The default setting is that PDA applications are disabled from writing to flash memory. In other words, 
priority is given to communication with the PlayStation 2. 
If flash memory writes are enabled for PDA applications, the PDA may stop communications with the 
PlayStation 2 to write to flash. Thus, the result from sceMcxSync() may repeatedly give "-12: PDA was 
not detected". 
 
• Real-time clock 
Sets the PDA's internal real-time clock (year, month, day, hour, minute, second, day of the week). 
The day of the week is automatically calculated. 
 
Return value in result of sceMcxSync(): 
Table 8-16 
Value Macro Result 
0 sceMcxResSucceed Successful 
-12 sceMcxResNoDevice PDA was not detected

 PDA Library - PDA Control Functions    8-23 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcxShowTrans             
Begin data transfer display     
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmcx 2.0 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceMcxShowTrans(  
 int port, Port number 
 int slot, Slot number 
 int dir, Transfer direction (0: PDA -> PlayStation, non-zero:  
PlayStation -> PDA) 
 int timeout) Timeout time to stop display if no request to end transfer 
display is received (in seconds for the launcher application) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
When saving a PDA application file, this function is called before opening the file to avoid alternate sector 
processing (to save the PDA program to contiguous memory). 
sceMcFormat() from libmc performs alternate sector initialization, so sceMcxShowTrans() should not be 
called when formatting the memory card (initialization of alternate sectors will be prevented and formatting 
will fail). 
When this function is called, a "file transfer control callback" is generated by the PDA kernel for the running 
PDA application. The data transfer display is displayed on the PDA's LCD screen in the subroutine 
registered in the "Start/Stop time setting for display of file transfers from PlayStation" in the PDA's "User 
callback settings (swi 1)". The timeout setting is used to allow the PDA application itself to stop the transfer 
display in cases such as when the PlayStation 2 is accidentally reset. In normal operations, the transfer 
display is cleared by calling sceMcxHideTrans() after the file transfer is completed. 
(For details on the operations that PDA applications need to perform, please refer to the "PDA Kernel 
Specification".) 
Return value in result of sceMcxSync(): 
Table 8-25 
Value Macro Result 
0 sceMcxResSucceed Successful 
-12 sceMcxResNoDevice PDA was not detected

Library Introduced Documentation last modified

Calling conditions

Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
Changes the thread priority of mcxserv.irx, the IOP module of the PDA library. Possible priority settings are

Table 8-30 
Value Macro Result 
0 KE_OK Successful 
-403 KE_ILLEGAL_PRIORITY Thread priority value exceeds valid 
range 
Notes  
The thread priority can also be set using sceSifLoadModule() when mcxserv.irx is loaded. For example, if 
mcxserv.irx is loaded as shown below, the initial thread priority will be 100. The thread priority string should 
be expressed as a decimal value. 
unsigned char *param = "thpri=100"; 
sceSifLoadModule( "host0:/usr/local/sce/iop/modules/mcxserv.irx", strlen(param)+1, param);

8-28    PDA Library - Library System Functions     
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
sceMcxGetErxEntries 
Get library entries 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmcx 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
void *sceMcxGetErxEntries (void);  
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets a pointer to the SceErxLibraryHeader structure which is needed to register the libmcx 
library in liberx. 
If the SceErxLibraryHeader structure returned by this function is passed to the 
sceErxRegisterLibraryEntries() function of liberx, the statically linked libmcx library can be called from an 
ERX module (can be dynamically linked).  
When this function is linked, the entire implementation of libmcx will be linked via the library entry table of 
liberx. 
In some cases, this may also increase the size of the program. 
Return value 
The entry library for libmcx is returned. 
See also 
sceErxInit()

 Multitap Library - Multitap Control Functions 9-5

sceMtapPortClose 
Close a port for the target multitap 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmtap 1.4 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceMtapPortClose(  
int port) Port number 
Controller port: 0 or 1 
MEMORY CARD slot: 2 or 3 
Calling conditions      
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Closes a port that was opened as a multitap connection destination. 
Physically, a single multitap is connected to controller port 0 and MEMORY CARD slot 2 (or controller port 
1 and MEMORY CARD slot 3) but internally these operate as independent multitaps. 
Thus, the ports opened with sceMtapPortOpen() should be closed separately. 
Return value 
Table 9-3 
Value Result 
1 Request was accepted 
Other than 1 Request was not accepted 
See also 
sceMtapPortOpen() 
 

===== PAGE 378 =====

9-6  Multitap Library - Mu ltitap Control Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3 -  Device Libraries 
sceMtapPortOpen 
Open a port for the target multitap 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmtap 1.4 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceMtapPortOpen(  
 int port) Port number 
Controller port: 0 or 1 
MEMORY CARD slot: 2 or 3 
Calling conditions      
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Specifies a port that is to be monitored as a multitap connection destination.  
The IOP driver only recognizes ports that were specified by this function as multitap connection 
destinations, and routinely polls them. 
Physically, a single multitap is connected to controller port 0 and MEMORY CARD slot 2 (or controller port 
1 and MEMORY CARD slot 3) but internally these operate as independent multitaps. 
Thus, when a multitap is used with both the controller and PS2 memory card ports, they should both be 
opened. If they are not, only slot A will be enabled. 
Return value 
Table 9-4 
Value Result 
1 Request was accepted 
Other than 1 Request was not accepted 
See also 
sceMtapPortClose() 
 
 

===== PAGE 379 =====

Gets information about whether or not the multitap is connected to an opened port. This function uses a 
RPC to ask the IOP for the latestinformation. 
For an unopened port, "No multitap" is returned, regardless of whether the multitap is connected.

PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3 -  Device Libraries 
sceMtapGetInfo 
Check multitap state 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmtap 3.0 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int sceMtapGetInfo(   
 int port, Port number 
Controller port:  0 or 1 
Memory card slot:  2 or 3 
 int *connect, Pointer to location where multitap connection state will be 
stored 
(*connect == 1:Connected, 0:Not connected) 
 int *slot_number) Pointer to location where number of slots belonging to 
connected multitap will be stored 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt-disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets information indicating whether or not a multitap is connected to an open port. If a 
multitap is connected, this function also gets the number of slots belonging to the multitap. The information 
is stored in *connect and *slot_number, respectively. *connect and *slot_number can also be set to NULL 
when the respective information is not required. 
This function does not use RPC. Instead it transfers the required information from the IOP using SIFDMA 
and accesses it only by referencing memory. Consequently, the function always finishes immediately 
regardless of IOP load. 
For a port that is not open, "no multitap" is returned regardless of whether a multitap is connected. 
Return value 
Table 9-6 
Value Result 
1 Request was accepted 
Not 1 Request was not accepted 
See also

sceMtapChangeThreadPriority     
Change IOP thread priority 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmtap 2.0 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int sceMtapChangeThreadPriority(

 int priority_low); Priority of SIF interface thread 
Calling conditions      
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
This function changes the thread priority of the mtapman.irx IOP module. Both of the following must be 
specified: 
priority_high main thread 
priority_low SIF interface thread 
The main thread is executed once per frame. The SIF interface thread is executed when any of the 
following are executed. 
sceMtapPortOpen() 
sceMtapPortClose() 
sceMtapGetConnection() 
sceMtapChangeThreadPriority() 
The thread priority can also be specified when mtapman.irx is loaded. This function cannot be run unless 
sceMtapInit() has already executed. 
Return value 
Table 9-7 
Value Result 
1 Successfully changed 
Other than 1 Failed

sceMtapGetErxEntries 
Get library entries 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libmtap 2.8 July 14, 2003 
Syntax 
void *sceMtapGetErxEntries (void);  
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled or enabled state) 
Description 
This function gets a pointer to the SceErxLibraryHeader structure which is needed to register the libmtap 
library in liberx. 
If the SceErxLibraryHeader structure returned by this function is passed to the 
sceErxRegisterLibraryEntries() function of liberx, the statically linked libmtap library can be called from an 
ERX module (can be dynamically linked).  
When this function is linked, the entire implementation of libmtap will be linked via the library entry table of 
liberx. 
In some cases, this may also increase the size of the program. 
Return value 
The entry library for libmtap is returned. 
See also 
sceErxInit()

scePadEnterPressMode 
Set controller to pressure-sensitive mode 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 1.2 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int scePadEnterPressMode(  
 int port, Controller port number 
 int slot) Slot number (fixed at 0, except when using a multitap) 
Calling conditions 
Valid only when scePadGetState() is scePadStateStable. 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Sets the controller of the specified port to pressure-sensitive mode. Actually, to switch the controller to 
pressure-sensitive mode requires several frames, and the switch is performed asynchronously. 
Determine when processing ends either by using scePadGetState() to monitor the controller connection 
state or by using scePadGetReqState() to get the execution result of the request.  
When the controller enters pressure-sensitive mode, the controller ID changes to 0x79. 
Return value 
Table 10-3 
Value Result 
1 Request was accepted 
Other than 1 Request was not accepted 
See also 
scePadExitPressMode()

scePadExitPressMode 
Exit from pressure-sensitive mode 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 1.2 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int scePadExitPressMode(  
 int port, Controller port number 
 int slot) Slot number (fixed at 0, except when using a multitap) 
Calling conditions 
Valid only when scePadGetState() is scePadStateStable. 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Causes the controller of the specified port, which is already in pressure-sensitive mode, to exit from 
pressure-sensitive mode. 
Actually, several frames are required for the controller to exit from pressure-sensitive mode, and the mode 
switch is performed asynchronously. 
Determine when processing ends either by using scePadGetState() to monitor the controller connection 
state or by using scePadGetReqState() to get the execution result of the request.  
When the controller exits from pressure-sensitive mode, the controller ID returns to 0x73. 
Return value 
Table 10-4 
Value Result 
1 Request was accepted 
Other than 1 Request was not accepted  
See also 
scePadEnterPressMode()

scePadPortOpen 
Begin communication with the controller 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 1.1 April 28, 2003 
Syntax 
int scePadPortOpen (   
 int port,  Controller port number (0 or 1) 
 int slot,  Slot number (fixed at 0, except when using a multi tap) 
 void* data) Work buffer, store at 64 byte alignment  
(The required size is defined by the constant

scePadSetActAlign 
Send details of actuator parameters to the controller 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 1.1 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int scePadSetActAlign(   
 int port,  Controller port number 
 int slot,  Slot number (fixed at 0, except when using a multitap) 
 const unsigned char* data) Sent details of actuator parameters (6 bytes) 
Calling conditions 
Valid only when scePadGetState() is scePadStateStable 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Notifies the controller of the locations where the actuator parameters are stored in the send buffer, by 
writing the actuator numbers in a 6-byte array. 
In the following example, send buffer offset 0 is used as actuator no. 0, offset 1 is used as actuator no. 1, 
and other data are not used. (The actuator numbers are stored in valid locations, and unused locations are 
set to 0xff.) 
Table 10-7 
Offset: 0 1 2 3 4 5 
Data contents: 0x00 0x01 0xFF 0xFF 0xFF 0xFF 
 
Since communication with the controller is carried out when this function is executed, other requests 
cannot be received for several frames.  
Completion of processing can be monitored using scePadGetState() or by checking the 
scePadGetReqState() result. 
Details of the actuator parameters set by this function become ineffective if the controller is disconnected or 
the controller mode is changed. 
Return value 
Table 10-8 
Value Result 
1 Request received 
Other than 1 Request not received

10-10  Controller Library - Controller Control Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
scePadSetActDirect 
Send actuator parameters to the controller 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 1.1 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int scePadSetActDirect (  
 int port,  Controller port number 
 int slot,  Slot number (fixed at 0, except when using a multitap) 
 const unsigned char* data) Starting address of transmit data 
6 bytes of transmit data should be provided 
Calling conditions 
Valid only when scePadGetState() is scePadStateStable 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Sends the transmit data to the IOP for operating an actuator. The transmitted data is sent to the controller 
during the next VBlank interrupt.  
In addition to specifying the send buffer with scePadSetActDirect(), controlling the actuator requires using 
scePadSetActAlign() to notify the controller regarding which offsets in the send buffer should be treated as 
actuator parameters. 
The data length accepted by the actuator can be determined with scePadInfoAct(). With the DUALSHOCK 
2, the data lengths of actuator number 0 and 1 are 1 bit and 1 byte, respectively. 
Thus, if scePadSetActAlign() is used with a parameter for actuator number 0 at offset 0 of the send buffer 
and a parameter for actuator number 1 at offset 1, sending a 0 or 1 at offset 0 and sending values of 0-255 
at offset 1 will allow the actuators to be controlled. 
An actuator will be stopped when its parameter is 0 and will run faster for larger values. 
Table 10-9: DUALSHOCK 2 actuator settings 
small motor 0=stop, 1=run 
large motor 0-255   larger values give higher speed 
Return value 
Table 10-10 
Value Result 
1 Successful transmission 
Other than 1 Failure

 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
scePadSetMainMode 
Change controller mode / lock changeover switch 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 1.1 March 29, 2002 
Syntax 
int scePadSetMainMode(   
 int port,  Controller port number 
 int slot,  Slot number (fixed at 0, except when using a multitap)

controller mode 
 int lock ) Lock/Unlock analog switch 
0, 1: keep current lock/unlock status 
2: Unlock 
3: Lock 
Calling conditions

Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
Switches controller modes, and switches between the locked and unlocked states of the mode switch 
button on the controller's main unit. 
When this function is executed, other requests cannot be received immediately, and controller button 
information will not be available for several frames. The completion of processing should be checked using 
scePadGetState() or the result of scePadGetReqState(). 
In addition, when the controller mode is switched, previously set actuator settings become invalid. 
Return value

Other than 1 Request not received

Get execution result of asynchronous function 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified

 Controller Library - Information Acquisition Functions  10-13 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
scePadGetSlotMax 
Get maximum number of controller port slots 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 1.4 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int scePadGetSlotMax (  
 int port) Controller port number (0 or 1) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled and enabled states) 
Description 
This function finds the maximum number of slots on a multitap connected to a port that was opened with 
sceMtapPortOpen(). It returns 1 if no multitap is connected. If the port has not been opened, the function 
still returns 1 even if a multitap is connected. 
Note that there may be a discrepancy between the information obtained from this function and information 
obtained from the sceMtapGetConnection() function because of timing related to the multitap connection. 
The multitap connection status and number of slots can also be obtained at the same time by using the 
sceMtapGetInfo() function, which is implemented in the multitap library. 
Return value 
Returns the maximum number of slots for the multitaps connected to the specified controller ports. 
 

===== PAGE 396 =====

10-14  Controller Library - Information Acquisition Functions 
PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
scePadGetState 
Get controller connection state 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 1.1 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int scePadGetState(  
 int port,  Controller port number 
 int slot) Slot number (fixed at 0, except when using a multitap) 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler  
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (does not depend on interrupt disabled and enabled states)  
Description 
This function gets the status of a controller connected to an open port. 
When the port is opened, the IOP’s padman.irx module independently monitors the connection and gets 
information about the controller. Several communications are required to update the controller status, 
therefore it takes several frames before this processing is completed. During this time button status is not 
available and requests from the application cannot be accepted. However, the application can use 
scePadGetState() to check the status of padman.irx processing. If scePadGetState() returns 
scePadStateStable or scePadStateFindCTP1, the application can obtain button information from the 
controller. 
Note that when a multitap is connected, the controller may be detected in slots 1 to 3 before the multitap 
itself is detected because of differences in sampling frequencies between this function and the 
sceMtapGetConnection() function. 
Notes 
With models like the SCPH-50000 or DTL-H50000 which have a PlayStation 2 DVD remote controller 
infrared receiver built into the console, when the remote controller's game-play function is set to "ON" from 
the browser's Set Up menu on the console, input from the remote controller will be accepted as if it were 
input from a digital controller connected to controller port 1. As a result, when the controller is removed, 
scePadStateDiscon will not be returned for controller port 1, and the application will not be able to detect 
that the controller was removed. 
Return value 
Table 10-13: Controller connection state  
Value Macro Result 
0 scePadStateDiscon Controller is not connected 
1 scePadStateFindPad Controller was not found (processing continuing) 
2 scePadStateFindCTP1 Detect ed the CTP 1.0 controller 
5 scePadStateExecCmd Communicating with controller 
6 scePadStateStable Detect ed the CTP 2.0 controller 
7 scePadStateError  Communication error detected 
 

===== PAGE 397 =====

 Controller Library - Information Acquisition Functions  10-15 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Among the states listed above, scePadStateFindCTP1 and scePadStateStable are the only ones in which a 
request from an application can be accepted or the button state can be obtained.

Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe

Table 10-14 
term Return value 
InfoActFunc Function number (1: continuous rotation vibration) 
InfoActSub Subfunction number (1 : low-speed rotation, 2: high-
speed rotation) 
InfoActSize Parameter data length  (0: 1 bit (on/off only), 1 or

argument is ignored. When the controller is not in READY state, or some error has occurred, 0 is returned.

 Controller Library - Callback Setting Function  10-25 
 PlayStation 2 EE Library Reference Release 3.0.3  -  Device Libraries 
Callback Setting Function 
scePadSetDmaCallback 
Register DMA termination callback function 
Library Introduced Documentation last modified 
libpad 3.0 December 1, 2003 
Syntax 
int scePadSetDmaCallback(  
 int (*func)(int)) Entry address of callback function 
Calling conditions 
Cannot be called from an interrupt handler 
Can be called from a thread 
Not multithread safe (must be called in an interrupt-enabled state) 
Description 
This function registers the callback function that is called when a SIFDMA transfer from the IOP ends. The 
SIFDMA transfer occurs when button and other information is sent during communication with the 
controller. 
Registering a callback function overrides the previously registered callback function. Registration can also 
be canceled by specifying NULL as the argument. 
The IOP frame counter is passed to the callback as an argument of func. 
Notes 
Since func is executed as an interrupt handler, it cannot call other APIs of the controller library. Be sure to 
perform processing such as starting up threads as needed. 
Return value 
The entry address of the previously registered callback function is returned. 
 

===== PAGE 408 =====
